C. Fred's decisions waiting

  1. [FIXED ON 2.8 11-11 — b7cd26242 (AICHECKNONET · AIPINWAIT · AIFEWFEAT), certified 07:53; 2.7e replay → Play next, with Fred's yes — see handoff v111] AI ROUTE BUILD DIES IN MOAB — OUT OF MEMORY (Fred 11-10 22:07). A RIDER HIT THE SAME ERROR ON THE RELEASED 2.7e (Fred 22:14, working with that user) — a live rider-facing fault: first in line for an emergency 2.7e update once the cause is traced. Recorded on Droid 1 22:10–22:12 (GroupTrackSpike/_data/routecrash_2026-11-10_2210.txt; that tablet runs the 2.8 build — the log shows GTSCHEMA): trailhead Slickrock; the route search read the trail network (RouteExplorer RESTRICTEDNEVER line) three times in 4 s (22:11:45.7, 48.4, 49.6); the heap went 54 MB → 131 MB in one second, then 151 MB, then hit the 256 MB limit at 22:12:01 — java.lang.OutOfMemoryError, FATAL, the app killed and back to the Android screen. Not caused by the 2.8 work (GtRoute is 2.7e's search, ported as is). CAUSE FOUND IN THE CODE (11-10 22:30; Fred 22:16: "sounds to me like the search is queuing multiple times" — it is): the trail network for the search area is built THREE times AT ONCE, each a full copy of Moab's network, and none can reuse another's because the shared cache is only filled when a build FINISHES. (1) The plan summary's checks (RouteModule.refreshSelection, AIFAILCHECK-2026-11-03 "connected trails from the trailhead" → GtRoute.connectedMiles → buildGraph) run in a background thread whenever the start, distance or FILTER changes; (2) the same LaunchedEffect is keyed on the STEP too, so tapping FIND MY RIDES (the step moves to SEARCH) starts the checks AGAIN; (3) FIND MY RIDES itself (explore → buildGraph). The threads are never cancelled and buildGraph has no lock. One Moab network took the heap 54 → 131 MB; three at once pass 256 MB. In 2.7e since the 11-03 AIFAIL checks. Fix options for Fred: (a) ONE network build at a time — a lock on buildGraph, so a second caller waits and then gets the finished network from the cache (the checks and the search use the same box, so the search would not build at all); (b) the checks run only when the plan changes, not when the step changes; (c) a newer check cancels an older one. (a) alone removes the triple copy; (a)+(b) also removes the wasted work. WORKED THROUGH ON THE DATA 11-10 22:40 (Fred 22:20: "we only did one build request"; 22:22: no devices tonight — "simulate these runs"): (1) the recording's "38 trails routed on their open parts" matches ONE search box only — Slickrock (38.5815, -109.5195) with a ride ceiling of 80 mi (box = ceiling / 3 = 26.7 mi each way, 53 x 53 mi): on the 11-08 database copy with Fred's FILTER that box reads 7,791 trails, ~382,000 points (cut 34 at 75 mi, 40 at 85 — only 80 gives 38). (2) One build of that network holds every point as a pair of doubles plus a cell index (touch / spread maps) — roughly 200–250 bytes a point = ~75–90 MB; the recording shows one build taking the heap 54 → 131 MB (77 MB). (3) ONE TAP, IN THE CODE: the plan summary (step 6) starts the checks' build #1 (RouteModule line 1012: LaunchedEffect(selKey, step) → refreshSelection → connectedMiles → buildGraph); FIND MY RIDES sets the step to 7 — the same panel is still on screen, its effect key changed, so the checks start AGAIN (#2) — and explore starts the search's own build (#3). #1 had not finished, so the cache was empty and #2 and #3 built their own. All three use the SAME box, filter and trail count — the same cache key. (4) Three × ~80 MB + the app's ~50 MB > 256 MB = the OutOfMemoryError at 22:12:01. Before 11-03 (no checks) the search built once and fitted. With fix (a) the same tap builds ONCE (#2 and #3 wait and take the cached network). The log-line patch inbox/graphtrace_2026-11-10/graphtrace_2026-11-10.py (dry run passed, NOT applied) confirms it on a device when one is available. Second recording 22:28–22:31 (Droid 1, same file name — it overwrote the 22:10 crash recording; the figures above were taken from it first): the run reports "Distance: 55-80 miles · Search area: 53 x 53 mi · Trails read: 7,854" — the 80-mile ceiling and box worked out above. No crash this time and NO network-read line at all: every request (the checks four times, the search once) took the network the app had already cached before the recording began — one copy in memory fits; the crash needs the builds to start before any has finished. The "error screen" Fred saw was the search's no-rides result, not a fault: "Stopped: 3 features here, but no ride of 55-80 miles fits them" (the checks: 40 mi of trail connected to Slickrock on Fred's FILTER, 2,383 junctions in 5 networks). A fix is a behaviour change: on 2.7e-support as an emergency update, and the same in 2.8 — Fred decides.

F. After the 2.8 base is running (behaviour changes — one at a time)

  1. The state-import rerun notice should persist until the state is reimported (Fred 11-12: a job that processes unsuccessfully must never be killed without notification). Today it lasts one launch — killedImportArea is @Volatile in memory only and the banner shows only in the state picker's detecting / confirm / list phases; after that only the killed file in imports/history/ remains. Candidate fix: derive the notice from the newest killed manifest so it stands until that state is reimported. Decide with G2f (StartupHousekeeping, DbAudit) — the port itself changes nothing.
  2. An explicit normal-end marker on the import manifest. Completion is inferred from the rows today; a marker would make "ended normally" readable rather than deduced. Raised 11-12, not decided.
  3. Whether the in-session Retry belongs in the state-data queue at all. 2.7e has it, so the port keeps it; Fred's design is that a failed state is rerun, not resumed. A product question for after the port.
  4. GroupTrack on Android Auto / CarPlay (Fred 11-11 21:45–21:48): "android auto / CarPlay compatible devices are much cheaper than an android or iPad tablet, everybody has a phone. makes barrier to entry cheaper, but apple compliance and cert is an issue." What it takes: a PHONE build (CarPlay runs from an iPhone, not an iPad — the shared code carries over, the screens need phone layouts); the navigation-category entitlement (Apple) / navigation app review (Google); the car screen uses the platforms' templates — the map plus a few driving controls (next turn, the group's carts, the route), planning stays on the phone. After 2.8. Fred's research 11-11 21:56: off-road apps qualify without turn-by-turn (onX Offroad and Trails Offroad run on CarPlay) — CarPlay: the Navigation entitlement (com.apple.developer.carplay-maps), CPMapTemplate in free-driving mode (a live map, no navigation session), at most 4 map buttons, must work fully offline with no network popups; Android Auto: the NAVIGATION category + MAP_TEMPLATES, a full-screen map without routing cards, at most 4 actions, an explicit pan button, handle geo intents (Play review NF-6); both: setup (downloads, imports, radio pairing) only on the phone. The engineering cost to weigh: both car screens need the map drawn NATIVELY into the car's surface / window — GroupTrack's map is MapLibre GL JS in a web view, so the dash view needs MapLibre Native (Android and iOS) reading the same vector + elevation stores.

  22. AI route search: only NEW routes (Fred 11-08, design agreed): exact duplicates of a route already on the device are always blocked; the route request carries the % of duplication to accept over an existing route, decided by the rider on each build; each ride's duplication % is shown in its description (so riders learn what, e.g., 75% looks like); blocking duplicates lets lower-scoring rides (other direction, fewer features) rise. Also: route planning should open Trails AND Routes (it opens Trails today).
  23. "NOT A TRAIL" mark on a track (Fred 11-04, "my own personal hot button" — NH road testing makes trails that do not exist). Defined 11-10 18:24: "not a trail" trails move into the Restricted category, as the map FILTER has it; they do not display, and the mark removes the "Rider" trails made from the track so they are not continually recreated and removed. Where (Fred 11-10 18:27): it is a change of the trail's category from "Rider" to Restricted, made in the trail's detail panel, where Rename etc. already are. READY.
  24. [DONE ON 2.8 11-11 — AIFEATFLOOR, 7c36038f1: one feature is enough] AI ROUTE SEARCH: FEATURES SCORE A RIDE, THEY DO NOT GATE IT (Fred 11-10 23:02: "we score based on features.... there should be no ceiling or floor on features"). Found 11-10 23:00 on Droid 1 (Slickrock, 31–80 mi, motorized only): 3 features in reach, the best start reaches 2 of them but they cannot share a ride ("tested ceiling 1") → 0 combinations → 0 rides at ANY distance. Today's search (2.7e, GtRoute.explore) builds a ride ONLY as a tour through 2 or more features (a ride needs a feature pair; the "ceiling" caps how many a ride may hold), so too few features = no ride. Fred's rule: build the loops / point-to-point rides of the asked distance from the trail network, then SCORE them by the features they pass — a ride with no feature is valid, just scored lowest; no minimum and no maximum number of features. Goes with GROW FROM THE TRAILHEAD (below) as one change of the search method, certified by re-running the saved AI recipes. Also: the "no rides" message must say why, never read as a distance problem.
  25. [11-11: still a box (the orange perimeter shows it); the connected network is now drawn green (AICONNECTED); growing from the trailhead not built] AI ROUTE SEARCH: GROW FROM THE TRAILHEAD, NOT A BOX (Fred 11-10 22:38: "we used to on an 80 mile trail go 40 miles out and build from the trail inside that 40 miles back to the trailhead, not the other way around. it reduces the number of trails evaluated"). Today the search reads EVERY trail in a box (ceiling / 3 each way: 53 x 53 mi at 80 mi) and builds the whole network — at Slickrock 7,854 trails in 5 separate networks — though a ride can only use the network connected to the start (40 mi of trail, 579 junctions). Growing out from the trailhead along connected trails, up to half the ceiling in TRAIL distance (40 mi at 80), only ever loads trails a ride can use: a far smaller network, and no memory limit on rides like this one. A change of the search method — certified by re-running the saved AI recipes (as step 13) and comparing the rides; done after the 2.8 base unless Fred moves it.
  26. Snap to the known trail / route (Fred 11-04): 30 passes over the same ground leave 30 trails though he is always within 50 m of the known trail — (A) live snapping while recording and (B) matching when trails are made from tracks; first step agreed: measure the pass spacing on Droid 1 against RiderTrailScanner's 20 m NEAR_M. Goes with the new snap-to scan method above.
  27. Classify trails speed (Fred 11-01; 11-10 18:24: "I thought we sped up the classify page size already — it was processing every trail rather than the new trails in that batch"). VERIFY in the code that the batch classifies only its NEW trails; close it if so.
  28. Filter restore after a route build (11-05; defined 11-10 18:24): launching a route build forces the map to display Trails and Waypoints; on EXIT from the route build, put the FILTER back to what it was before the build — manual or AI build alike. READY.
  29. KMZ / GeoJSON from email (11-05; defined 11-10 18:24): the import from an email prompts for what the file is — "does the file represent trails?" — so the right processing option is picked (the same question as the one import). READY (goes with the import rules above).

⭐ 2.8 STEP 17 G2e-2 — the state-data QUEUE certification: scope, method and what went wrong (11-12, scope approved by Fred 18:40)

What this group certifies: QUEUE PROCESSING ONLY. Fred 11-12 18:39 — step 17 breaks the service-level functions into groups and certifies groups, so that when a job or screen is tested later everything underneath it is already certified, and certifying like services together surfaces common problems and common fixes. This is not an end-to-end process test.

  • The fetch is STUBBED, and that is correct. The internet transfer is a platform service, certified separately in G7 — server and catalogs ("http → the shared client (ktor); cert = the requests made, against a replay server"), each occurrence.
  • The database writes are already certified — steps 1–16 (15f agency trail import: Droid 572,503 SAME / 0 DIFF, iPad 116,299 SAME / 0 DIFF; 15g OSM; 16c/16d save-by-area).
  • What was wrong with the first attempt: it used invented rows r1–r4. The rows must be real manifest rows, because the queue branches on their real values. Fred 18:02: bring the data over by where and how it is stored at the end of the process, then run the process again in simulation on the real data. (The first run also stalled at scenario C — moot, the harness is replaced.)

The input — real rows, already on the Mac

Pulled 11-06 from Droid 1: GroupTrackSpike/_data/droid1_2026-11-06/imports/history/ — 20 real manifests (Utah ×17, New Hampshire ×2; all process_state=killed from the 11-02 test session — the rows are what matter). Extracted to GroupTrack/inbox/step17g2e2f_2026-11-12/real_rows_2026-11-12.json: Utah 9 file rows (1 pbf, 1 gpkg, 1 land, 6 agency) and New Hampshire 6. Row keys: id, name, process, fetch_kind, state_slug, url, dest, status, source_id, bbox.

What the real rows exercise that invented rows did not:

  • The pbf and gpkg are both on download.geofabrik.de — the "never two files from one server at once" rule actually fires, and interacts with the PBF gate.
  • The 6 agency rows each have a distinct source:<id> pseudo-host — real parallelism up to maxAtOnce (1–4).
  • Mixed starting statuses — pending / stopped / completed in one manifest: completed must stay out of pending, stopped must re-queue. Real recovery state.
  • 8 non-file rows in the same array (_classify, the per-source rows, geofabrik_utah) — the sort must handle rows with no fetch_kind (ORDER default 5).

ownership appears only in the three manifests before 12:47 on 11-02. Fred 18:40: it is polyline-based, not protobuf — it was a separate pass. It has no entry in the queue's ORDER map (gpkg 0, land 1, agency 2, pbf 3) and does not need one; every manifest from 13:10 on has only the four kinds ORDER covers. Use a post-12:47 manifest and do not "fix" ORDER for it.

What is compared — OLD (2.7e) vs NEW (shared), same rows

A pure simulation: no download, no explode, nothing live touched. One shared runner drives both sides; the fetch is stubbed per row by the scenario. Compare the whole sequence, not just the end state: (1) start order; (2) concurrency at maxAtOnce 1, 2 and 4; (3) which row was held because its server was busy (the geofabrik pair); (4) the PBF release point; (5) every status transition per row in order, with current_step and _stop; (6) final row states and run()'s return (true only when every row completed, false on cancel); (7) the flows waitingOnRider, active, maxAtOnce at each settle point.

Scenarios, all on the real rows: A all pending at 1 → 2 → 4 · B the manifest's own mixed statuses as-is · C one row's fetch fails → settles, waitingOnRider true, others continue → Retry it · D Stop a running row mid-fetch · E Cancel → in-progress stopped, returns false, saved data kept · F all already completed → true, nothing started · G a retry/stop for an unknown id → ignored · H New Hampshire's 6 rows.

Pass criteria (step 17 inventory §4, unchanged)

TEST-ONLY generated OLD copy with counted substitutions (any miss writes nothing) · compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64 before the build · Droid OLD = NEW, 0 DIFF, report written, not aborted · iPad 0 DIFF against the Droid's own report with equal input fingerprints → "cross-device COMPARED" · every DIFF traced to its cause · two clean runs before it is called certified.

The one real finding — the stop() thread (fix before the cert run)

2.7e closed the download connection on its own thread: Thread { HomeStateImportController.abortDownload(row) }.start(). The new shared stop(id) calls abort(row) directly, and the Android front door passes abortDownload with no thread — so a slow close now runs on the caller's thread and could block the UI. The shared file's own comment says "Android runs it on a new Thread, as 2.7e"; the wrapper does not match it. This is an unintended behaviour change in a port that is meant to change nothing. Wrap it, then scenario D certifies the real behaviour. Where the Stop button calls stop(): still to confirm.

Design doc: claude/GroupTrack_2.8_step17_G2e2_queue_cert_design_2026-11-12.md. Group definition: docs/GroupTrack_2.8_step17_inventory_sequence_2026-11-11_v1.html (G2, sequence item 17-1; Fred 11-11 21:20).

⚙ THE STATE IMPORT — how it actually works (settled 11-12 by reading the code; durable)

  • The queue does not stop on one failure. A failed row is marked failed, the other rows keep running, then the queue settles and waits for Retry or Cancel (waitingOnRider). That is 2.7e behaviour — commit 502509847 (11-02): "RETRY on a failed file, CANCEL IMPORT ends the run cleanly and keeps the saved data". Whether the in-session Retry should exist at all is a product question for after the port, not a port question.
  • A failed state is rerun, not resumed (Fred 11-12 17:37–18:02): an internet loss or server timeout is not recoverable in place. Downloaded files are retained, so a rerun is cheap — that is why the automatic restart was dropped. The JSON state exists to support recreation and recovery of trail data: you must know what was successfully downloaded to recreate it.
  • Startup: StartupHousekeeping → HomeStateImportController.sweepManifests, every launch. Any manifest with an unfinished row is stamped process_state=killed + killed_at, then moved to imports/history/ (stamp first, then move — load-bearing). Never resumed. Any abnormal end reaches this path — app crash, process kill, or the user ending the import — because the test is on the manifest's own rows, not on how the app ended.
  • The rerun notice exists (KILLEDIMPORT-2026-10-02): the sweep sets killedImportArea; HomeStatePickerScreen shows "The [state] state import did not finish and was removed. Select a state and process again."; beginImport() clears it. Fred's rule: a job that processes unsuccessfully must never be killed without notification.
  • The step list is per area, not fixed. The manifest's sources array is written in full before processing starts: one row per state the bbox touches, the catalog sources, the per-file rows (fileRows), and the classify stage. An area crossing three states produces many more steps than one small state with no USFS data. The test is job-step execution, not counts.
  • Where the data lives (confirmed from the real manifests' own dest values, under getExternalFilesDir(null)): 
    /storage/emulated/0/Android/data/com.grouptrack.android/files/GroupTrack/
    osm/<slug>/<slug>-latest.osm.pbf           (pbf)
    osm/<slug>/<slug>-latest-free.gpkg.zip     (gpkg)
    states/<slug>/padus_<ST>.jsonl             (land)
    states/<slug>/agency/<source_id>.jsonl     (agency, one per source)
    imports/import_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<state>_state.json   (the dated record)
    imports/history/                                 (completed and killed records)
    The retained files are not dated — "latest" means each download replaces the previous one. The record carries the date.
  • Two queues, not one. The state-data queue (StateDataQueue / GtStateDataQueue) has no priorities — it orders by file kind (gpkg, land, agency, pbf), one file per server at a time, the pbf last. The map-tile queue (ConvoyDownloadQueue) is a different job and is the one with priorities, lower first: DELETE 0 · PROMOTED 1 · CORRIDOR 2 · AREA 3 · REFRESH (RFSH) 4 — RFSH is the lowest and runs last; PROMOTE sets 1.

🧮 GtMath — Android's math on the iPad (the platform rule found today; it governs all shared code)

  • How it was found (07:17–07:37): the 11-09 15e iPad report had 13 rows about times. Fred 07:17: "we have prided ourselves on resolving every difference" — find it, don't accept it. MATHPROBE (a TEST-ONLY probe: the haversine's every step, Droid vs iPad, bit for bit) showed the FIRST differing step was cos (one last bit: droid …5006, iPad …5005), then everything after it. Apple's libm and Android's give different last bits on some inputs; a distance one bit different moves a track property, a rider-trail cut and anything hashed from it.
  • The rule: shared code never calls kotlin.math sin / cos / asin / hypot / pow / tan / atan / sinh / ln — it calls GtMath. Android: GtMath = java.lang.Math, unchanged by construction. iPad: GtMath = Android's OWN algorithms in plain Kotlin:
    • cos / sin: FreeBSD msun (Android's libm) — GtMathPort; asin / hypot: fdlibm — GtMathPort. Proof MATHPROBE/MATHPORT: 6,000,000 Droid values (C, S, A, H 1,500,000 each), iPad 0 DIFF (08:13).
    • pow: Arm optimized-routines pow (what Android's libm compiles), with platform.posix.fma for the fused steps — GtMathPow. First tried x·x for pow(x, 2.0): MATHPOW (08:32) showed Android's Math.pow(x, 2.0) is NOT x·x on 1,266 of 1,500,000 (Android's pow is not correctly rounded) → gtmath2b replaced it with the port: 0 / 1,500,000 (09:50). Apple's own pow would differ on 2,305.
    • tan / atan / sinh (+ the exp / expm1 they use): fdlibm, line for line from JDK 21 FdLibm.java — GtMathFdlibm (common code; its huge-argument reduction shares GtMathPort.kernelRemPio2, made internal). StrictMath = the Droid on these, proven by MATHTILE.
    • ln: Arm optimized-routines log (N = 128 table, POLY 6 / POLY1 12, with fma where clang contracts one expression) — GtMathLog (iosMain). glibc aarch64 = the Droid.
  • MATHTILE (16a, 16:57): the tile math the save-by-area writes run, on real inputs (every track point's latitude and every 7th trail's, 400,000 latitudes, + seeded random): Apple differed on tan 158,960 / 400,000, ln 647, sinh 29,336, atan 6,943, lat2tile-before-2^z 25,040, stored box edge 15,899. Fred (AskUserQuestion): "Port them to GtMath (Recommended)". After the port (MATHTILE2, 17:10): T, C, L, Y 400,000 each and SH, AT, O 112,782 each — 0 DIFF.
  • Math.toRadians / toDegrees = × the constants (GtJava.toRadians / toDegrees), proven again by MATHTILE (Y and O kinds).
  • Kept TEST-ONLY until GtCert is removed before the 2.8 release (CODE RULE 3): GtMathProbe.kt (modes mathprobe, mathpow, mathtile) and the iosTest MacTests (GtMathPortMacTest, GtMathPowMacTest, GtMathTileMacTest, GtMathTile2MacTest).

🔎 Other findings today (platform / harness lessons)

  • D14 — the 1970 file-date rule (15e): 2.7e sets a track file's time to its earliest <time>; java.io.File.setLastModified refuses a time before 1970 (IllegalArgumentException "Negative time"), Apple's file call accepted 1582 → the "Time zoo" test track differed. Shared code now refuses exactly as Java does, on every platform (Android unchanged).
  • The startup-job guard lesson (15f fix B, the 11-08 lesson again): run 12:03 had 111,741 DIFF — all carto_code / trail_properties rows of OLD's fresh copy — because the 2.7e startup job GtUtahPrivate.startup (MainActivity: waits 60 s, rebuilds land areas, runs step 8 = OwnershipReclass) ran ON OLD'S SWAPPED COPY during the cert (12:04:30 "step 8: classifying 54827 rows"). Now stood down by GtCertGuard like the other startup jobs (TEST-ONLY line). Rule: every startup job that touches a database must be on GtCertGuard before a cert runs.
  • Cross-device fingerprints leave out android_metadata (Android's own table; the iPad has none) — 15f fix B, again in 15g (the crafted GeoPackages DROP android_metadata + VACUUM).
  • Masked sizes: a snapshot's size is the size of the MASKED text (the Droid's instants carry microseconds, the iPad's fixed clock none — same sha, different size). Snapshots skip SQLite side files (-journal / -wal / -shm).
  • 15f fix A: kotlinx refuses JsonUnquotedLiteral("null") — a JSON null is JsonNull (run 11:49 aborted there).
  • Android's double endTransaction after 2.7e's extent abort throws IllegalStateException("Cannot perform this operation because there is no current transaction.") — 2.7e reports that as its failure; the port reproduces it (GtOsmExtract's Tx). 2.7e's insertRow binds an Int as TEXT — reproduced.
  • okio: openZip / InflaterSource / Inflater are common code; zip entries listed in central-directory order (GtImportConverter.zipEntryNames, now internal).
  • THE HASH TRAP (D13): StringBuilder.append(Double) and string templates are Java's Double.toString on Android, NOT on the iPad — GtJava.jd for every double in WKT / text.
  • Java HashMap iteration order (16b): 2.7e's queueSquaresNamed joins z12 squares into boxes through two HashMap<Long, Box>; the order it walks them IS the order the boxes are queued (the job order in download_queue.json). The iPad's HashMap walks differently → GtJavaHashMapLong reproduces JDK 8+ HashMap (hash = h ^ h>>>16, table 16, ×2 above 0.75, tail insert, lo/hi split on resize, bins in order). Not reproduced: a tree bin (needs 9+ keys in one bin with a table ≥ 64 = 49+ runs of squares in ONE z12 row) — counted, the 16c cert will report it.
  • gt_build_v2 flaw (found 17:29): its "code clean (matches the commit)" check does not count UNTRACKED new files — the 17:28 build at 7f06a478b compiled the four 16b files (installed, not yet committed) and still said clean. Harmless this time (committed unchanged as 264f3d186), but a build can contain code git cannot reproduce. Fix: count untracked files under app/ and grouptrack-shared/ (gt_build_v3, with the process review).
  • Read verdicts from the report files (not the terminal paste) — still the rule; twice today a command was run before its install step (1–2 s "builds"): always install → compile → build → run.

🗺 16c — the certification design (written 11-10 so the next session starts at once)

What it proves (plan v10 step 16, Fred 11-09): from the same area or recipe, OLD (2.7e) and NEW (GtVectorQueue) produce the same queue requests — boxes, cells, counts, labels, order, the download_queue.json text. A pure write: no download, nothing touches the live queue. No extra checks invented ("we don't plan for lightning strike").

  1. OLD side (TEST-ONLY, generated by a make_old_copies_16c script, every substitution counted, any miss → nothing written): VectorTilesCertOld = a verbatim copy of convoy/VectorTiles.kt with DownloadQueueManager.enqueueArea( → DownloadQueueCertOld.enqueueArea( and GroupTrackStorage.dir("recipes") → the cert's recipe folder; DownloadQueueCertOld = the two enqueueArea overloads copied verbatim from ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt with init(context), _queue.value = current, saveQueue(), startNextIfAvailable() → nothing, and val current = _queue.value.toMutableList() → the cert's collected list. quickEstimate / segmentCells / QueueEntry.toJson are 2.7e's own pure code, called as they are.
  2. Ids and clocks: 2.7e's QueueEntry defaults (UUID.randomUUID, System.currentTimeMillis) — each entry takes one of each, in creation order; the cert renumbers OLD's in append order (q1, q2 …, createdAt 0) and gives NEW the same sequence through GtIds / GtClock. Every other field compared exactly.
  3. The file text: OLD = JSONArray(entries.map { it.toJson() }).toString(2) (Android's org.json); NEW = GtQueueJson.fileText. Compared as text (sha256 + length per case; the first DIFF of each kind written in full).
  4. DOWNLOAD_ZOOM: the Droid checks ConvoyConfig.DOWNLOAD_ZOOM == 18 (STOP otherwise); the cert's platform answers 18 on both sides.
  5. Inputs, real data only, written INTO the Droid report so the iPad replays exactly the same ones (hex bits for doubles): (a) every track of the cert copy of the spatial DB (cert15c_input) → squaresFor, then queueSquaresNamed one track at a time (the corridor path) and ALL tracks together with their names (the multi-corridor path — the hardest test of the HashMap order); (b) Droid 1's newest recipe (grouptrack_maps_*.gtmaps) → boxesOf / recipeBoxes, squaresInBox + queueSquares (the restore / conversion path) and queueFromRecipe; (c) the boxes of Droid 1's current download_queue.json (READ only — Fred's real save-by-area areas) → enqueueWithElevation; (d) quickEstimate and segmentCells on the same boxes at zMax 18 (a box big enough to split into several cells is among Fred's real areas or the recipe).
  6. Verdict: Droid OLD = NEW 0 DIFF, then the iPad = the Droid's report 0 DIFF, cross-device COMPARED (gt_cert_v24, as v23's rule). Expected cost: a few minutes per side (no database writes).

Known limits of the port, to watch in 16c: the tree-bin case of the HashMap (counted); a corrupt recipe's error text ("Could not read the recipe: …" — the JSON reader's message differs from org.json's; the outcome is the same).

⭐⭐ POST-CONVERSION — the import rules: 4 formats × 4 artifacts (decided 11-08 17:27–17:59, Fred) — NOT built; step 15 ports the import AS IT IS

Added 11-09 (Fred) — part of this design, also deferred to after the 2.8 base (moved here in v217 from the 11-09 decisions, which went to COMPLETED_TASK_2026-11-10 with the old START HERE):

  • Import split rules (16:47): a track file imports as a track; its waypoints become separate waypoints and are not kept inside the track. Trail files the same: lines become trails, points become waypoints (closes this design's open item "points in a trail file"). Reason: the filters cannot handle combined entities. GPX already works this way today.
  • The 15e shared module is the IMPORT PREPROCESSOR (limited cases, 2.7e rules) + the GPX writer; this two-job design expands it (16:52–16:59).
  • Rider trails off the screen's path: the track add returns at once; the rider-trail scan runs as a background task (the map refreshes when the trails land).
  • A new rider-trail scan method (17:30–17:49, for correctness — the current one leaves gaps a second pass fills): walk the track; where snap-to finds an existing trail, follow it; where it does not, create trail until snap-to finds one again and join there. One pass, no gaps. Check: snapped length + created length = the whole track. Proven by running old and new on the same tracks.

Status (Fred 17:59): decided, NOT built. Done AFTER the 2.8 conversion. Step 15 ports the import AS IT IS today, with today's rules, line for line and certified identical. This design is applied afterwards as its own change, on the shared code, once for Android and iPad. Do not re-decide anything below; it is settled. Fred: "it will focus the components and make refactoring easier when we are done."

Source of the facts about today's code: inbox/step15_2026-11-08/GPX_identify_and_write_2026-11-08.md (every line cites file:line).

The frame (Fred 17:32)

  • 4 file formats: GPX, KML, KMZ, GeoJSON. 4 artifact types: waypoints, routes, tracks, trails.
  • The target of every import is a GPX with each artifact already identified; the GPX importer then reads it by fixed rules.
  • Only the import process is in scope.

Definitions (17:39–17:41)

  • Track = what was driven: a recording, points with times (usually elevation).
  • Route = what was planned: no times.
  • Trail = a line someone means as a trail. GPX has NO standard trail value ("does it exist?" — not as a standard; in practice trail GPX files are untimed <trk> with nothing marking them). Only the person importing, or GroupTrack's own mark, can say "trail".

THE DESIGN: two import processes (Fred 17:56–17:59)

Both end in the SAME GPX importer (one writer, one set of rules). Only the preprocessing differs. The panel's "Are these files trails?" question goes away; the Map menu gets two entries. Fred: "aligned in the proper sections of the maps and grouped together; it simplifies the panel for imports."

1. Import trails

  • Any format (GPX, KML, KMZ, GeoJSON).
  • Every line becomes a trail — timed or not, single or multi-piece.
  • The preprocessor writes GroupTrack's trail mark (<type>grouptrack-trail</type>) on every line, GPX included (a GPX -> GPX preprocess), then the GPX importer writes them. The importer's only trail rule is the mark.
  • DECIDED 11-09 16:47 (Fred) — closes the open item: points in a trail file (trailheads, parking) become separate WAYPOINTS; only the lines become trails. The same for a track file: it imports as a track and its waypoints become separate waypoints, not kept inside the track. Reason (Fred): "our filters could not handle combined entities gracefully." The full rule: every file is split into single-type objects on import — points are always waypoints; lines become a track, route or trail by the matrix; no object carries another inside it. GPX already does this today (each <trk>, <wpt>, <rte> to its own type).
  • Named 11-09 (Fred 16:52): step 15e's shared module is the import preprocessor (limited cases, 2.7e rules) plus the GPX writer — the post-conversion job EXPANDS the preprocessor's rules (the matrix, the split, the two menu entries); the writer, hashes and storage underneath do not change.

2. Import tracks, routes and waypoints

  • Any format. The content decides; no question.
Artifact GPX KML / KMZ GeoJSON
Waypoint <wpt> Point Point / MultiPoint (each point one waypoint)
Route <rte>; a <trk> WITHOUT times LineString (no times) — "line strings are routes. period." (17:44) LineString / MultiLineString without times
Track a <trk> WITH times gx:Track / gx:MultiTrack (times) a line with times (coordTimes)
Trail never — except a line carrying our grouptrack-trail mark (a GroupTrack trail export) never never
Not imported — Polygon (an area) Polygon

Rules for every import

  • A point is always a waypoint (17:37).
  • The waypoint's description is saved (today it is thrown away).
  • The waypoint type comes from the file's type or icon (GPX <type> / <sym>, KML icon, GeoJSON type / marker-symbol) through a MAPPING TABLE of source values to our 10 types (trailhead, fuel, gate, hazard, scenic, water, camp, parking, rally, other); else "other". Not guessed from the name.
  • A multi-piece line without times becomes one route per piece ("X", "X (part 2)") — never joined (joining draws a straight line between pieces that do not touch) (17:48).
  • A multi-piece recording (gx:MultiTrack) becomes ONE track with one segment per piece (17:48).
  • Elevation is kept wherever the file has it.
  • No guessing from names, folders or styles. Geometry and times decide; only the Trails import overrides.
  • Resync tracks (the my_tracks folder sync) is NOT an import: it keeps GroupTrack's own track files as tracks.

To build with it (from the 11-08 reference, section D)

  • The mapping table — drafted from the real files in inbox/step15_testset_real/ for Fred to correct.
  • GroupTrack's single-trail export (GtGpx.trail) writes the trail mark (today it does not, so a trail re-imports as a track — contradicting the panel's own text).
  • The converter: keep elevation; gx:Track / gx:MultiTrack (times, space-separated gx:coord); routes as <rte>; multi-piece rules above; descriptions kept.
  • Emailed / tapped KMZ and GeoJSON reach the import (today only GPX and KML are recognised by their text).
  • Keep originals that went through the converter (Fred 18:23: post-conversion, not now). Today the import panel deletes a picked KML from Download after moving it into the app (the staged copy reads as <kml), leaving only the lossy -Converted.gpx — the KML's elevation and gx:Track recordings are then gone if the rider has no other copy. KMZ and GeoJSON originals are kept only by accident (the text check cannot read them).
  • Retire the importer's unused asTrails switch and the old direct KML import (ConvoyTrackOps.importTrackFile, no callers since ONEIMPORT 11-04) after the callers check.
  • The older <type>trail</type> (11-03) form: decide whether it is still read.
  • Tests: one case per cell of the table, each with its known right answer, plus the Hillbilly Hills KMZ/GPX pair.

History found on the way (for the record)

  • The original track import read KML directly (ConvoyTrackOps.importTrackFile: GPX or KML, Placemarks split into my_tracks .kml files, dates from <when>). ONEIMPORT 11-04 replaced it: KML/KMZ/GeoJSON always go through the converter to GPX; only GPX reaches the database import. Leftovers still live: my_tracks lists .kml, the earliest-time reader reads <when>, emailed KML is recognised.
  • Today's converter on "No": lines -> tracks (elevation dropped; a MultiGeometry split into several tracks); points -> waypoints type "other"; descriptions and attributes lost; gx:Track and polygons dropped silently. No test exists for this path.
  • No code identifies tracks or trails from a Gaia or onX value; their only effects: a route's <desc> becomes its narrative, <extensions> stripped, date formats.

⭐⭐ 2.8 STEP 22 — ride time zone, calendar entry (decided 11-08, Fred) — after steps 15–21 (the iPad app working)

THE FUNCTION, in one line (Fred 11-10 19:48–19:49: "it is one function"): the ride's time zone and calendar entry — ONE function (Fred 11-10 19:49): the ride form takes date, start time and time zone the way a calendar entry does (time picker, zone selector; rides.time_zone, schema v2); the organizer's zone defines the event and each receiver's calendar converts it, with every receiver told the event was adjusted; a calendar entry is CREATED on the organizer's device at ride creation and on a rider's device at ride import, and the invite carries the .ics for riders without GroupTrack; the ride's meeting waypoint (name + GPS) is the entry's LOCATION. The 11-10 additions (create on ride import too; date/time stored as a calendar event; the waypoint in LOCATION) are part of this one function, not separate items.

Status (Fred 11-08 12:44, 12:46): moved out of step 14 to STEP 22 of release 2.8, after steps 15–21 (the iPad app working). Nothing of it is built. Build it on the SHARED ride code (com.grouptrack.shared.ride), once, for Android and iPad together. Do not re-decide anything below; it is settled.

Why it moved, and why nothing is lost

  • "Do ride conversion once" (Fred 08:55) is satisfied by the certified port (commit 9657a8ac1) and the switch. 14d is a feature added later on shared code: still written once, reaches both platforms.
  • It is a database change (schema version 1 -> 2), kept out of this stretch ("steady the ship").

Fred's decisions (all final)

  1. GroupTrack riders get the ride package; everyone else gets the route GPX and a waypoints GPX as two separate attachments; everyone gets a calendar entry with the meeting waypoint's GPS in the location (08:27–08:55).
  2. The ride's time is defined in the ORGANIZER's time zone. It goes out as an exact moment; each receiver's calendar converts it to their own zone. Calendars shift automatically when the phone changes zone (Fred 09:01).
  3. Awareness is the key (09:03): every receiver is told the event was scheduled in another zone and has been adjusted.
  4. The ride form captures date, time and zone "just like a calendar entry", validated (12:38): date picker, TIME PICKER (no free text), time-zone selector preset to the organizer's current zone and changeable.
  5. Calendar entry for GroupTrack receivers: the app opens the phone's calendar pre-filled; the rider taps Save. No calendar permission (my default, 12:38 — Fred did not object).

Design

Database (shared GtSchema, new step, schema version 1 -> 2)

  • rides.time_zone TEXT — IANA name (America/Denver, America/Phoenix, America/Los_Angeles; Navajo Nation = America/Denver).
  • New rides store start_time as HH:mm (24-hour). Old rows keep their text (e.g. "8:00 am") until next edited.
  • NO stored moment/UTC column: the moment is always computed from ride_date + start_time + time_zone, so it can never disagree.
  • Old rows: time_zone NULL = "read in the organizer's zone" (show as stored, no conversion notice).
  • Certify with the schema check: FRESH, UPGRADE from 1, AGAIN identical (CertSchema / CertSchemaMacTest, gt_schemacheck).

Ride form (ConvoyRideCreateScreen)

  • Date picker (as now) + Material3 time picker + "Time zone" selector under it.
  • Zone list: US zones; Arizona and "Navajo Nation (observes daylight saving)" listed separately; any IANA zone reachable.
  • Label: "Start time (Mountain – your time zone)".
  • Cannot save without date, time and zone.
  • Editing an old ride: parse "8:00 am", "08:00", "8am", "8:00AM", "13:30"; if unreadable, the picker opens empty and the form asks for the time.
  • CODE RULE 1: saveRide/updateRide get the zone as a required parameter.

Ride package

  • ride.timeZone added; formatVersion unchanged; older app versions ignore it. Import stores it into time_zone.

Send (Android Intent; all text generation in shared code)

  • ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE: .convoy package + route GPX (route only) + waypoints GPX (trailhead + ride waypoints) + .ics.
  • Attachments named after the ride, like the .convoy today.
  • Invite email keeps today's text, plus a short section for riders without GroupTrack: open the two GPX files in your trail app; add the .ics to your calendar.

The .ics (RFC 5545, shared)

  • VTIMEZONE for the ride zone (Phoenix has no daylight saving); DTSTART;TZID=<zone>:YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS — NOT a bare UTC time, so calendars know it was scheduled in another zone.
  • DTEND default = start + 4 hours (to confirm with Fred when built).
  • UID = rideId@grouptrack; LOCATION = trailhead name + "(lat, lon)" from the same trailhead the package uses (recipe anchor), else the ride's first waypoint; GEO:lat;lon.
  • DESCRIPTION, fixed and true for every receiver: "This ride is scheduled for 9:00 AM Mountain Time (UTC−7 on this date). If your calendar is in a different time zone, the time shown has been adjusted to your time zone." — abbreviation and offset worked out FOR THE RIDE DATE; then the leader's notes.
  • Escaping and 75-octet line folding. No zone or unreadable time -> all-day entry with the stored time text in the description.

GroupTrack receiver (import)

  • After import: "Add to calendar" -> phone calendar pre-filled (ACTION_INSERT; on iPad EventKit UI), event time zone = ride zone.
  • Description computed ON THE RECEIVER'S PHONE: "This ride is scheduled for 9:00 AM Mountain Time (UTC−7). It has been adjusted to your time zone: 11:00 AM Eastern, 2 hours ahead." Nothing when the offsets are equal at the ride moment.
  • A notice written at import stays as written; the calendar time itself always moves with the phone.

Ride panel (live)

  • Each time it opens: "Ride destination is 1 hour behind your current time zone (UTC−7 vs your UTC−6)", worked out on the rider's phone from its current zone.

The rules that keep the difference right (Fred 08:57: the biggest risk is misinforming the zone difference)

  1. Work out the difference AT THE RIDE'S DATE AND TIME, never today's. (Today Utah and Arizona are both UTC−7; a July ride is Utah UTC−6 vs Arizona UTC−7.)
  2. Store named zones, never fixed offsets.
  3. Shared code, kotlinx-datetime (0.7.1 is in libs.versions.toml; add it to grouptrack-shared).

⭐ 2.8 STEP 14c — how the ride certification was run and judged (11-08) — the method for every later step

Result: rides CERTIFIED twice in a row vs 2.7e on copies of the frozen data (runs 2026-11-08_1225 and _1229: 2042 same; only the 6 labelled lenient-JSON lines differ). Commit 9657a8ac1 (pushed). The app still runs 2.7e's ride code; the switch is next.

The method (keep using it)

  • gt_cert_v14.sh <build> rides (v12, v13 superseded). Records the Droid log LIVE during the run (the map's DetailGate spam flushes the buffer) to $CERT/logcat_<build>.txt(.all).
  • Certified only when: # rides done written, zero unlabelled DIFFs, zero SpatialDb/SQLite warnings FROM GROUPTRACK'S OWN PROCESS (v13 wrongly counted another app's warning).
  • Two clean runs in a row before calling it certified.
  • Don't open the Rides screen during a run (its heal writes the live rides folder).
  • 2.7e ride code stays live during certification; GtRidesAndroid is in its own file; the harness copies OLD results into the shared classes. The four wrappers are installed only at the switch.
  • The app is not debuggable (run-as fails); the harness copies its exported packages to GroupTrack/gtcert_rides_export/<old|new>/ for adb pull.

Harness lessons (STEP14C-B .. F)

  • B: one input only the old code can read (single-quoted "lenient" JSON) shifted every later line and the clock ticks: 201 false DIFFs. Fix: the lenient cases run LAST (case 8, labelled expected DIFF), and the compare re-syncs on keys (an extra line is one DIFF, not a cascade).
  • D: after every case the app's database handles must still be that side's copies, and open; otherwise the run aborts.
  • E/F: probes around each build (route id, narrative rows in the copy, readRouteNotes, routeRecipe, env recipe) and the frozen/copy file report (sizes, fingerprints, -journal/-wal) — this is what found the real cause.

THE REAL CAUSE of the "missing trailhead" (runs 0928, 1019, 1207) — STEP14C-G

  • The app's startup database repair (GtDbAudit.repair, 10 s after the screen opens) reads SpatialDbManager's spatial handle, then later the data handle, and ATTACHes one to the other to delete rows "pointing at nothing".
  • When a cert run swapped the handles in between, it paired LIVE spatial with the TEST-COPY data database and deleted the copy's 9 route notes for "broken ridge 11/04/2026 2" (a route Fred deleted from live).
  • The other order (copy spatial + LIVE data) would have deleted LIVE route notes / route names and live track files for anything newer than the frozen copy.
  • Fix (TEST-ONLY, removed with GtCertRun): GtCertRun.active is set when a request is taken; DbAudit.repairNow logs "DBREPAIR skipped: a certification run is using swapped database handles" and returns.
  • Live data checked 11-08 12:33 (copies in GroupTrackSpike/_data/live_2026-11-08): 22 routes (newest 11-05) all with notes, none with fewer notes than on 11-06; 131 tracks (newest 11-07 10:25) all with files. Nothing lost: nothing newer than the frozen copy existed, so even the dangerous order had nothing to delete.
  • Rule for any future test that swaps handles: startup jobs that read SpatialDbManager handles at two moments must not run during the test.

Port facts recorded

  • A recipe anchorName stored as JSON null becomes the trailhead name "null" in the GPX (org.json's optString). The port keeps it byte for byte; it belongs with the backlog's "null" trails item.
  • Single-quoted (lenient) ride JSON: 2.7e accepts it, the shared code rejects it ("That isn't a GroupTrack OG ride file.") — accepted as an expected difference (PORT_NOTES a3).

🔥 The fault of the day — area downloads stopped adding tiles (found 16:38, fixed and shipped by 17:41)

  • Symptom (Fred 14:11): offline maps missing around his area (Wilton → Methuen, Boxborough → Hooksett) though downloaded by area; a fresh area download "completed successfully" and nothing showed.
  • Evidence: the store copy (iPad export, 16:21) held 57,054 tiles — exactly the same as 11-06 11:30: the 11-05 recipe restore had added nothing. The 11-05 recipe DID hold the NH/MA boxes (12 of 72). The live trace of a fresh area download (16:37): REFRESH MODE … 923 stored tile(s) in bounds … downloaded=923 failed=0 for an area needing 947.
  • Cause: two changes that did not fit together — VECRFSH-2026-10-30 typed every VEC/DEM area job MAP_SOURCE_REFRESH; REPLACEFIX-2026-11-03 sent a MAP_SOURCE_REFRESH job with Replace = Yes down the REFRESH path (only tiles already stored). Replace became the default the same day. So since 11-03 every area download, track corridor, recipe restore and re-download after Remove area with Replace on fetched only what was stored — a new area came down empty, a removed area never came back, and every job said "completed, 0 failed". (Fred: "they were lost to a fault — area downloads were no longer adding new tiles.")
  • Fred's rule (16:44–16:47): Replace decides ONE thing — whether tiles already stored are fetched again and overwritten. Missing tiles are ALWAYS fetched. REFRESH in the queue is only a job's kind and priority (lowest, long-running).
  • Fix REPLACEONLY-2026-11-07: one line in ConvoyDownloadWorker (refreshOnly = false); the refresh branch is now unreachable (cleanup later). Verified on Droid 1 17:03: VEC 1,736 / 1,736, DEM 129 / 129, 0 failed, shown offline. 2.8 61f553fb6; 2.7e update d 0c9722359 = update c + this line only (git diff: one file), version code 7222 > c's 7221 — published 17:41. Release notes v78 + manual v137 carry it. Fred's maps rebuilt from the 11-05 recipe on the fixed build: all back (17:45).
  • Not affected: the 2.7e map conversion and the Emergency download (both queue with Replace off).

🧰 Harness lessons (11-06 — they govern every run from here)

  • Fred's method: run the old method, log it; the new method, log it; the Mac, log it — every filter element on and off by itself, over the WHOLE database. Heavy per-trail checks sampled (every k-th trail id ≈ 20,000 + the edge set ≈ 3,790 + the 200 largest).
  • Run on Android in the FOREGROUND (the cached-app freezer stalls a backgrounded run). gt_cert_v5.sh <build> [reads|writes|all]: the request file goes in BEFORE the force-stop (v4 lost 90 min on 11-06: Android's keep-alive restarted GroupTrack before the file existed), and the script STOPS if the run has not started within 10 s.
  • Never compare SQLite's own number text across platforms — Android's system SQLite and the bundled one print doubles differently. Compare exact bits, or Android's %g emulation (GtRead.cG).
  • Stream hashes (okio HashingSink) — the Droid ran out of memory (256 MB) building a 5,000-trail chunk as text.
  • Patch self-check: two of my patches (fix1, fix4) put a "//" note mid-line and commented out code. Every patch now checks that no "//" sits inside an edited code line and that braces / parentheses balance before it writes. Build commands use set -o pipefail so a failed build stops the chain.

📌 Decided / recorded 11-06 evening (Fred)

  • Riders are ~60% iPad, 40% Android — the iPad app is the majority platform; a non-inclusive community will not launch. Order: iPad app → installed base on both → the community launches on it.
  • Unified user base, platform independent (V3): (1) same data and behaviour — the shared model; ride packages and radio messages move with step 14; (2) one account across platforms — Apple requires Sign in with Apple (or an equivalent privacy login) when an app offers Google sign-in, so V3 needs one user with several login identities (Google, Apple), before the App Store submission; (3) one subscription — Play and the App Store bill separately; V3 records "active subscriber" whichever store billed.
  • Community principle (Fred 18:12): the community supports NON-GroupTrack users. Routes and waypoints travel as GPX (every riding app reads it), ride schedules as calendar invites (.ics). GroupTrack packages it better: maps along the ride offline, trails (incl. rider trails), the route on them, group tracking on the day.
  • V3 (AWS) is built through phase B and parked: minor DB tweaks + a commercial-grade web front end; the functionality to launch the community exists. Sequence: iPad first cut → V3 DB tweaks (aligned with the shared model's tables, plus identities + subscription) → the web front end.
  • Background work on the iPad: GroupTrack's background is mostly the DOWNLOAD QUEUE. Foreground: the queue runs as on Android. Background: hand the remaining downloads to iOS background transfers in BATCHES (many small tile requests are slow there); the queue state already lives in the database (download_queue), so nothing restarts. Long jobs (imports, land rebuild) run in the foreground and must be RESUMABLE — a design rule for steps 15 and 16. GPS recording with the screen off and the radio over Bluetooth are supported (background location / Bluetooth modes; Meshtastic's own iOS app proves the radio). ⚠ A Wi-Fi-only iPad has no GPS — cellular iPad or a Bluetooth GPS puck (open question for the manual / product).
  • Screens bet (Fred 17:58): the screens are already Compose (Compose Multiplatform keeps the API). Measured over 82 screen files / 29,322 lines: Context 245 uses / 59 files (mostly reaching the database — gone once the model is shared), Toast 124 / 15 (one shared gtToast), Intents / share / pickers 42 / 14 (a few platform helpers), WebView 44 / 8, BackHandler 12 / 5, AndroidView 4 / 3 (the map, the help viewer). ≈ 6 platform helpers written once + mostly mechanical edits; the risk sits in the map view, pickers / share, and screens that call SpatialDbManager directly (why the model moves first).
  • iOS beta launch kit — GATED (Fred 19:57): NOT shown until there are sample screens from the iPad app AND at least one working install on a real iPad (≈ step 18). Then: TestFlight (internal group first — up to 100, no review; external public link after Apple's beta review of the first build); a site sign-up "iPad / iPhone beta — tell me when" (name, email, device, home state, one privacy line); one featured teaser panel from the real screens ("Your ride, now on iPad"), tagged for the site report, + a Facebook teaser.
  • Four-part OVERVIEW — the manual standard (Fred 17:47, "memorialize the format"): every function's section opens with a framed OVERVIEW, in this order, each a bold lead-in and one or two short sentences: What it does. · How you use it. · What it means to you. · When to use it. It is the sales "overcoming the objection staircase" (right beside "always be closing"): feature → use → the rider's benefit → the moment they need it. Riders gave great feedback on the 24 Map menu overviews. In the app the menu shows OVERVIEW beside a function only when the link table has its own section; OVERVIEW opens the manual there and runs nothing. Route, Rides, MAP KEY, FILTER, Readout, Account get theirs AFTER the first iPad cut (Fred 17:46).
  • Maps: satellite imagery is too dense for offline use, and trails are hard to see on it (under trees, in shadow). DirtOS (Sean Connell) uses Mapbox satellite + 3D terrain — Mapbox bills per monthly active user with a free allowance, requires its watermark, caps offline size; DirtOS offline is in Pro ($49.99/yr). GroupTrack stays vector + elevation offline (no per-user map bill, no download cap), trails drawn from data. Satellite only ever as an online / small-spot layer.
  • Site report 11-06: ~100–130 people/day; Play taps ~2% overall but 16% from Facebook (the channel that works); the manual is the most-visited page (335 paths); best taglines "Turn a route into a ride" (10) and "Open a ride in one tap" (8). To act on: an iPad sign-up (gated above), tag every Facebook link (82 of 101 untagged). Manual readers who skip the home page: robots (heavy repeaters) + 355 iPhone/iPad no-referrer one-hit visitors still unexplained — run commands/manual_readers_check_2026-10-29b.sh (read-only, never run yet).

📋 After 2.7e — agreed with Fred 11-04 (design, not built)

  • SNAP TO KNOWN TRAIL (Fred 14:56–15:00: "30 paths over the same ground leaves 30 trails... we are always within 50 but we never draw along the known trail or route"; Fred votes A and B). Findings: (1) LeadEstimate.kt already snaps live at SNAP_M = 50 — but only to place the estimated lead marker when the lead is silent, never the recorded track; (2) RiderTrailScanner.kt already does B with NEAR_M = 20 m and feeds new pieces back as network, so repeat passes are SUPPOSED to leave one trail. First step: measure on Droid 1 — one stretch driven many times: count its rider trails, measure pass spacing. If passes are >20 m apart, widen NEAR_M (confirm Fred's "50" is ft or m); if not, find the bug (pieces not fed back between runs). Then: B (match at trail creation) → A (live map matching with a short delay so junctions are decided by the next 100–200 ft; keep raw + matched track; widen the tolerance with poor GPS accuracy). CPU is not the constraint (~1 ms per fix).
  • "NOT A TRAIL" mark on a track (Fred 08:08, 14:25, 14:55 "my own personal hot button... a lot of road testing in NH... a lot of trails that do not exist") — hides its trail, route planning skips it, Create trails from tracks never rebuilds it, the track is kept. Interim: Remove tracks and trails by area with Tracks ticked (loses the track).
  • Remove the remaining Meshtastic leftovers in the 2.8 separation (the handleMeshtasticUri path in MainActivity is now unreachable).

📘 MANUAL / QUICK START — UPDATE NEEDED (Fred 15:39: "make notes that manual needs updates for map display and filters")

  • ☐ FILTER — two rows replace the old LAND row:
    • SHOW TRAILS ON THIS LAND TYPE: Public · Private · Restricted — which TRAILS are drawn. Restricted = motorized trails (or their parts) inside wilderness / WSA, drawn red. Default: Public + Restricted on, Private off.
    • SHOW LAND AREAS: Public · Restricted — the striped overlays only; both on by default. No Private area (private = the plain map). Areas show from map zoom 9.
  • ☐ MAP KEY — new LAND section: Restricted trail (red line) · Public land (green stripes) · Restricted area (red stripes, other direction) · Private land (no stripes).
  • ☐ MAP DISPLAY — what the rider sees, bottom to top: public green stripes → trails → restricted red stripes (trails show through) → red restricted segments → labels / tracks / routes. A trail that enters a restricted area: its open part in its normal colour, the restricted part red. Roads along an edge or in a cherry-stem (within 15 m) stay normal. Non-motorized trails are never red (allowed in wilderness).
  • ☐ MANUAL TEXT — squares of open land inside a restricted area (Fred 15:49–15:51, approved): "Some restricted areas contain squares of land that are not part of them -- most often state trust land, set aside at statehood to support schools. GroupTrack shows these squares as public land (green stripes), not restricted. Being allowed on the square does not mean you can drive to it: if the only way in crosses the restricted area, there may be no legal motorized route. Check the managing agency before you plan a ride into one. Riding legally is the rider's responsibility." Background: Utah school sections (SITLA), granted at statehood; WSAs cover federal land only, so the state squares are holes in the WSA (PAD-US "Inholding" layer, no longer restricted -- INHOLDING-2026-11-02).
  • ☐ MANUAL + RELEASE NOTES — state data is now kept (Fred 16:02): "GroupTrack now keeps all the state data it uses to create and classify trails -- the trail and map files, the public-lands file and the agency trail data -- on your tablet. The space saved by the new maps makes this possible. A second import of the same state, or adding trail areas in a neighbouring state you have already loaded, now runs in a fraction of the time: nothing is downloaded again. Use "Download fresh data" on the import panel whenever you want the latest; GroupTrack also offers a refresh when a state's saved data is more than six months old." Measured 11-02: Utah import 30 min → 3 min 9 s with the data kept.

Why the estimate follows area, not gigabytes (Fred 20:34–20:55): the new map is ~22 tiles per z12 square (21 map Z12–14 + 1 elevation) + shared low zooms; the old maps at Z18 are ~16,000 tiles per square (5,461 × 3 layers). Gigabytes track area only when every area is the same depth: the heaviest user (110 GB + 129 GB, regions to Z18 everywhere, likely southern Utah) ≈ 1,100–1,400 squares ≈ 24,000–31,000 new tiles ≈ 8–10 min; Fred doubts it ("he downloaded tiles for days … we will see"). Formula for a quick check from tile counts: new tiles ≈ Z12 tiles of one source × 22; minutes ≈ Z12 tiles ÷ 145. Fred's own: 816,601 old tiles (3 sources) → 53,768 new.

Trails capped at 1,000 at every zoom (Fred 19:13: "lint trails down to 1000 largest") — Droid 2 sent 5,000 trails = 16.7 MB per pan, memory full (170 of 170 MB). Order unchanged: named first, then largest.

Radio findings (firmware 2.6.11 AdminModule.cpp): channel set → no reboot; owner → reboot only if changed; config → reboot except some fields, Bluetooth always; module config (serial) → always reboots and turns Bluetooth off; commit_edit_settings → Bluetooth off + save + one reboot. Wio L1 Pro: after the serial commit it stopped advertising > 2 min (the writer waits 60 s), came back on its own; serial off restarts in ~30 s. Changing a radio or a callsign needs the old node removed and forgotten in Android Bluetooth (Fred: configured the Wios as bare radios, forgot them, then all four radios talked). Droid 2's clock runs ~2 h behind (Mountain).

🔁 MIGRATION — ✅ BUILT 10-31 (b075e8ed1; as built in "10-31 AFTERNOON + EVENING" above — the screen, CANCEL, not held for internet, Emergency download replaces "Restart map conversion") — was: the design (Fred 10-30 15:17–15:55)

Satellite is gone (Fred 15:19: "satellite is gone. that was the purpose of this to eliminate google licensing and multiple maps."). After 2.7e there is ONE map: vector, with elevation underneath. map_sources.json ends with only vector_sources + elevation_sources. The SAT/VEC switch built today (GtMapEngine, map_engine.txt) is scaffolding for the Droid 1 / Droid 2 comparison only.

The process (Fred 15:23, 15:26):

  1. Build the recipe for recreation from the tiles on the phone — always the first step, whatever version the user came from. The tiles are the truth, so an old install with no recipe is covered the same way (Fred: "I would suggest rebuilding json as first step of the process").
  2. No tiles → it is a new install: go straight to the standard install process.
  3. Tiles found → convert: queue every vector + elevation job from the conversion recipe, then delete the old map stores (SAT, SAT_LABELS_*, TOPO, TOPO+). Queue-then-delete (agreed 15:34): the VEC/DEM downloads do not touch the raster stores, and a kill at any moment leaves either the old maps or queued jobs — never neither. (Supersedes the 10-29 "delete the raster directories first".)

Untouched by the process: tracks, trails, routes and waypoints (Fred 15:26) — they live in the spatial DB, not the map stores. VEC / DEM stores already on the phone (Droid 1) are kept; the recipe only fills gaps.

Recovery — belt and suspenders (Fred 15:34–15:54):

  • The queue is the restart (Fred 15:34: "if it is killed the queues should pick up where the conversion died. that was the purpose of the queues."). Jobs are persisted; nothing extra is built for restart.
  • The conversion recipe stands until the next daily recipe replaces it, and no daily recipe is written while the conversion's jobs are still in the queue (a recipe taken mid-rebuild records less coverage than the user had). Failures → resubmit. Fred 15:51: "as long as replace is no — no damage on re run." Verified in code 15:55: VEC/DEM jobs queue with refreshMode=false → the normal path, where ConvoyTileDownloader ~174 skips any tile MBTilesStore.hasTile already holds; only the refresh path sets forceOverwrite=true.
  • Maps › "Restart map conversion" (Fred 15:54, name agreed: "perfect. belt and suspenders"): requeues vector + elevation from the saved conversion recipe (not that day's), replace off, any number of times.
  • The conversion stays until the next release install (Fred 15:52; CODE RULE 3): an ungated startup entry point with no "done" marker. It decides from what is on the phone — raster stores present → convert; none → step aside. After a conversion the rasters are gone, so later starts pass straight through. A start killed between queue and delete simply runs again (pending jobs skipped by the queue, stored tiles by the downloader). Restart map conversion leaves with it.

Still open: does the conversion run silently on first start, or show a panel first ("your maps are being replaced, about N MB, Wi-Fi recommended")? The 10-29 design said a message, online only, Yes / Next start — not re-decided today.

New D items (Fred 20:48–21:24) — 2.7e list

ItemDecision
D30 trail nomenclatureOne word was doing three jobs (LAND Public/Private). Four independent fields: Access public · permit / pass (ride parks, paid areas) · private · unknown; Use = the existing map-key rows (OHV, Track, Forestry/access, Shape only, Rider trails, Unofficial; Hiking & biking, Hiking, Biking, Equestrian, Steps/bridge); Difficulty easiest · moderate · difficult · extreme (or none) — trails have none today, only the 1–5 stars on tracks; Source official (agency) · OSM · park map · rider trail. Filter's LAND row gains Permit / pass, shown by default. Open: is "Permit / pass" the riders' word (or "Ride park")? Values follow OSM tags where they exist (access=permit).
D31 import trailsFinal (Fred 21:08–21:24): a new Maps-panel item "Import trails (GPX / KML / KMZ file)" beside Import GPX. It runs the original import with one flag "this batch is trails" (Fred 21:11: "everything that runs now works … a switch in the code that says this batch is trails. does not touch anything else"); flag off = the original process unchanged. With the flag: every line of the file goes to trails as one group (system name + access asked once), names from the line or the attribute table's NAME + number, difficulty from Rating / <type>, source "park map"; points stay waypoints. KMZ/KML preprocessing: the import's file-type step identifies KMZ (.kmz or a zip starting PK with a .kml inside) and KML (.kml or text starting <kml), converts it to a temporary GPX (lines → <trk>, points → <wpt>) and launches the original import on it; the same front step serves Import GPX (flag off), Import trails (flag on) and "Open with GroupTrack". Fred 21:21: "the kmz could be anything" → the RIDER chooses by the menu item; the converter decides nothing. KML would also go through the converter (fixes <Placemark id=…> and blank names; one KML reader left).
D32a rider trail namesRider trails are inserted unnamed on purpose (RiderTrailWriter ~287, 09-07 "it doesn't have to be perfect"). Proposed: each rider trail takes its source track's name ("Gizmo (70)"); several pieces → "· part 2". The match is exact (rider-trail points are a subset of the track's points). D32b ("make trails from selected tracks") not needed once D31 exists.

Findings behind them

  • Hillbilly Hills (Kentucky) — a new rider "had no trails"; Fred: import your tracks and they become trails. Fred downloaded the park's map ~/Downloads/TrailsV3.kmz (295 KB; doc.kml 1 MB; Document "HBT Trails Latest", an ArcGIS layer export): 161 Placemarks, 171 LineStrings in MultiGeometry, bbox 37.239–37.304 N, 82.490–82.555 W; attribute table per trail: FID, Number, Label, NAME (e.g. Main Loop, Gizmo, Colonel Sanders, Butcher Holler Climb, Allegheny Overlook), Rating (Easiest 26, Moderate 44, Difficult 33, Extreme 20, Connector 37, Main Loop Easiest 1), miles. Why GroupTrack could not import it: (1) .kmz is not accepted (import takes .gpx/.kml); (2) the KML multi-track splitter matches only a bare <Placemark> — these carry id=; (3) names live in the description table, not <name>. Converted to GPX (161 named tracks) and imported on Droid 1 → 161 tracks + unnamed rider trails — the wrong door: a park's trail map came in as rides. Leave it as test data; when D31 exists, delete the HBT tracks (check the 10-24 open item: trails from deleted tracks have come back) and import the KMZ through Import trails.
  • GroupTrack never imported KMZ (code + git history). The KML/KMZ/GeoJSON handling Fred remembered is Meshtastic's own map-layers feature (feature/map/src/google/.../MapViewModel.kt ~408–483) — it only draws the file over Meshtastic's map; nothing reaches GroupTrack's DB.
  • GPX has no "trail" type — only wpt, rte, trk (+ free-text <type> and per-app <extensions>); KML none either. A gt: namespace (gt:kind=trail etc.) was proposed and set aside for the simple flag; revisit if GroupTrack exports trails. Note: the import strips every <extensions> block first (onX namespaces once broke the split).
  • Direction (Fred 21:24): "the possibility of local databases replacing our need to create every trail is pushed down to users and riding groups" — parks, clubs and riders bring trail maps in; with V3 sync (shape matching, first in wins, Source recorded) "our trail maps become very complete". Owed before sharing goes live: the owner's name and an "I have the right to share this" confirmation on any uploaded trail map; a "seen by N riders, last ridden" counter as confidence.
  • Kentucky state import on Droid 1 (~20:10), open observation: download size and target both bouncing 2–10 MB, restarting; Fred rebooted; afterwards it ran (record files/GroupTrack/imports/import_2026-10-29_kentucky_state.json, written 20:16). Not caused by 10-28/10-29 commits (none touch import/download). If it recurs: adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat -d -v time -s HomeStateImport:* OsmImportStage:* | tail -40 BEFORE rebooting.
  • Path correction: the app's folder has a GroupTrack level — imports: /sdcard/Android/data/com.grouptrack.android/files/GroupTrack/imports/; recipes: …/files/GroupTrack/recipes/ (the 10-29 notes said files/recipes/). The tablet's grep does not take \| — cat the file instead.

⭐⭐⭐ RELEASE 2.7e — VECTOR MAPS: one map source, OpenFreeMap tiles, MapLibre (decided 10-29 17:49–19:48, Fred) · BUILT 10-30 on Droid 1 · migration designed 10-30

STATE 10-30 EOD — BUILT on Droid 1 through 3D, carts and heading-up (commits cfa4de7dc → 9c18ef9e8). The plan, the migration design, the tester-build order and today's rejections are in SATURDAY 10-31 — START HERE at the top. What below is superseded by 10-30: (1) satellite as the alternative single source — rejected (Fred 15:19, "satellite is gone"); (2) the conversion deletes the rasters first — now queue first, then delete, and it starts by building the recipe from the tiles (an install with no tiles is a new install); (3) the vector corridor "draws the route's rectangle + ~5 miles" — now tight boxes over the full area (Fred 15:57); (4) elevation (contours/hillshade "not in 2.7e") — in 2.7e: a second, non-selectable source (AWS Terrain Tiles, DEM store, z0–12) always queued with vector; 3D works; (5) the step-3 interim "Google Hybrid until MapLibre" — done and passed: the map is MapLibre now.

PURPOSE: replace the raster map stack (Google Hybrid / Esri Topo / OpenTopoMap + label stores, Leaflet) with one vector map drawn by MapLibre — licence-clean, a fraction of the storage and download time, sharp at every zoom, rotation and 3D, and the same map page for Android, iPad and Mac. Fred 19:47: "this is no less intrusive than it was at the beginning … our area and corridor downloads will continue … all features come in the pbf downloads prepositioned, and we have a replacement service for providing map data within the same request and fulfil structure our downloads currently support. all with no changes in infrastructure."

WHY NOW: D29 — the Google sources (default slot 1) break Google's terms, and stored Google tiles are on every tablet. Fred 18:07: "goggle exposure is not small. it is embedded on every device." The 07-26 / 08-14 MapLibre direction (memory: grouptrack-vector-rendering) was already set; this makes it the next release.

SCOPE — Fred's five steps (19:13), in order:

  1. Add the vector map source to map_sources.json: OpenFreeMap, a fourth store directory beside SAT / TOPO / TOPO+, format pbf, z0–14, not a Leaflet map type. Check the downloader's tile validation and MBTiles metadata (format=pbf) before the first run.
  2. Rebuild the map in that fourth directory by finishing the recreate-from-recipe function: newest recipe → each box's lat/lon exactly as reported (Fred 18:32: "we need to download exactly the long and lat we report down to z14" — no recount) → AREA jobs z0–14 into the vector store; the existing queue manages itself (Fred 18:29). Test: Droid 1's recipe → Droid 2 (Droid 1 stays the reference on the current build).
  3. Remove TOPO and TOPO+ as map sources → one active source (Google Hybrid until MapLibre) and one map button fewer. Their files stay on tablets until step 5.
  4. Remove Leaflet, install MapLibre in ONE MAP's page — directly, on the MapLibre branch, tested on Droid 2 against Droid 1 (Fred 19:06: "just replace the leaflet with map libre once we have the tiles over. has to be better than taking it apart and making things coexist"). Rule: the JavaScript bridge stays identical — every function Kotlin calls (fitBounds, setView, showQueuedAreas, showSearchCenter …) and every Android.* call back keeps its name and arguments, so Kotlin does not change. Order: list the bridge contract → base map (stored vector, offline via addProtocol) → display trails/routes/tracks/waypoints (GeoJSON from SpatialDbManager, unchanged) → taps (queryRenderedFeatures) → search and fit → downloads (area drawing, route rectangle) → planning (pins, build, compare, create a ride) → saved view (+ bearing, pitch) → group riding display last (carts, lead track, check-in; the tick was removed in 2.7b). Fonts (glyphs) bundled in assets or names vanish offline. Style starts from an OpenFreeMap style (Liberty / Bright / Positron) adjusted to a topo look; one download can carry several looks (style switch, not a second source).
  5. Conversion routine (one-time, CODE RULE 3): message on first start of the new version, online only — "Updating your maps to the new format: old map images removed, N areas downloaded" — Yes / Next start → write today's recipe (it is built FROM the stores, so it must be fresh before anything is deleted; the recipe folder survives) → delete the three raster directories first, Google included (Fred 18:28: "once we change the method to vector then the images have to go … we will need to reclaim the space before downloading") → queue the recipe boxes as vector AREA jobs z0–14. Trails, routes, rides stay drawn throughout.

FRAME — what does not change: trails, tracks, routes, waypoints, rides and the spatial DB; snapping (Kotlin, against the DB — never snap or route against tiles: clipped, simplified, unstable ids); the Geofabrik import of trails, places and natural features (reference_points feeds RouteExplorer's scoring and "features this route passes" — the tiles' names are for drawing only); the download queue, MBTiles, recipes, area and corridor downloads (for a vector source the corridor simply draws the route's rectangle + margin, ~5 miles, a named setting — Fred 18:23: "leave corridor in place and it simply draws the rectangle by itself"); ride import downloads the ride's maps as now (follow-up to measure: carry the ride's vector tiles inside the .convoy file).

THE MODEL AFTER 2.7e: one map source per rider (Fred 18:12: "we are not doing both. one map source"). Default and target = vector. Satellite was argued both ways (18:12–18:22); Fred's resting position 18:21: "moving maps to one source which is vector based or sat is exactly the process we have now without the three sources" — satellite (USGS imagery, public domain, by corridor) remains possible as the alternative single source; vector needs no corridor at z14. Google, Esri, TOPO, TOPO+ and the two Esri label stores retire.

SOURCE — OpenFreeMap (Fred 19:40: "we have enough to do without creating and managing infrastructure"): free public instance, no registration/key, "no limits on map views or requests" (README), z/x/y vector tiles in the OpenMapTiles schema to z14, so the existing queue downloads them unchanged; attribution required: "OpenFreeMap © OpenMapTiles, Data from OpenStreetMap" (MapLibre shows it); publishes full planet MBTiles weekly — the self-host fallback with the same z/x/y addresses, so the app's process never changes (Fred 19:21: "whatever we are doing we need to do it from day 1. no changing processes in the middle"). Not confirmed: its terms do not mention offline/bulk storage either way → send the operator a note before release. Street names, places, water, land arrive inside the tiles (transportation_name etc.) — no PBF kept on the tablet, no reprocessing per area (Fred 19:43).

THE RECIPE (read 18:30, ConvoyMapRecipe.kt): recipes/grouptrack_maps_YYYY-MM-DD.gtmaps in the app's external files dir (/sdcard/Android/data/com.grouptrack.android/files/GroupTrack/recipes/), written once a day at startup, 60 kept; JSON {version:1, created, slots:[{slot, source, zMin, zMax, tiles, boxes:[{n,s,w,e,tiles}]}]}; boxes from DownloadQueueManager.deriveRecipe — a quadtree over STORED tiles, empties dropped, ≤50,000 tiles per box, lat/lon. The restore was deliberately left unbuilt (08-12); its rule: replay as DownloadType.AREA + DownloadPriority.REFRESH — a refresh-typed replay downloads nothing and completes at zero looking successful. The recipe screen already lists "RECEIVED (Downloads)" → Droid 1's recipe can drive Droid 2.

STATE 10-29: designed only. Measurements owed first: a southwestern-Utah vector area on Droid 2 — size, download time, look at z15–17 vs Esri/Google on roads Fred knows (estimate: a western state at z0–14 = a few hundred MB; a rider's areas less).

NUMBERS that drove it: Droid 2 08-02: SAT 4.24 GB + label stores 0.46 GB; Bar 10 corridor ≈107,000 tiles across five stores; zooms 15–18 ≈ 98% of a raster download, and vector stops at 14. Server (if self-hosting ever needed): EC2 8 GB disk (5.4 GB free, shared with the website), 1 GB RAM, 1 vCPU, AWS CLI 2.33 present, no credentials (needs an IAM role), IMDSv2 token required.
