Also on the table, not in 2.7e: contours and hillshade from USGS 3DEP / elevation tiles; public land from PAD-US (public domain) — "am I on public land?"; private parcel lines (onX's headline feature) need licensed county data — separate decision. onX for reference: its own tiles compiled from public GIS + its guides, licensed imagery, Mapbox renderer (MapLibre is the open fork of Mapbox GL).

iPad / Mac: MapLibre GL JS is a web library — the same map page runs in the iPad/Mac WKWebView; per platform only the native pieces differ (tile serving via the URL scheme handler, the SQLite DB, BLE). Doing this on Leaflet would have baked Leaflet into the layer iPad and Mac wait on.

REJECTED — AND WHY:

  • Self-hosting on AWS (S3 + CloudFront, a PMTiles file, a PMTiles range reader) — worked out 19:17–19:31 (cost pennies; downloads would bypass the EC2 server), dropped 19:37–19:40: Fred does not want to build and manage infrastructure for what a free service provides. Kept as the fallback only, fed by OpenFreeMap's weekly planet file.
  • Cutting states out of the file — Fred 19:23: "no cutting states out. our promise was 50 states."
  • OSM's own vector server — its policy prohibits offline/bulk downloading.
  • Protomaps public build as a live source — one big file (not z/x/y), not meant as a production source; would have needed a reader and a later switch (breaks "no changing processes").
  • Grandfathering Google through the Meshtastic fork (Fred 17:31) — Meshtastic's Google map uses the licensed Maps SDK with its own API key; a fork carries the code licence, not Meshtastic's agreement with Google; GroupTrack bulk-stores raw tiles from an unofficial address. The corridor argument (17:33, "targeted, not bulk") helps with volunteer servers' volume concerns, not with Google's no-storage rule.
  • Moving trails into the vector tiles / routing on tiles — tile geometry is clipped and simplified, ids unstable (07-26 rule).
  • First-launch area picking / riding-area boxes / trails cut into grid squares on a server (18:16–18:21) — Fred: first-launch setup "will be painful"; trails by state are cheap and done once ("we have a process that works"); maps were the heavy part and vector at z14 removes it.
  • Download vector, then delete raster — Fred 18:28: delete first to reclaim space; the images are useless after the change.
  • A second test map screen / Leaflet and MapLibre coexisting (19:01–19:06) — replaced by a direct swap on Droid 2 with Droid 1 as the reference; the maplibre-gl-leaflet plugin (vector under Leaflet) noted only as an emergency interim — it gives no rotation or 3D.
  • A Mac browser test page — Fred 19:01: no spatial DB there; test on the Droid.
  • Satellite + vector both — Fred 18:12: one source, never two.

📣 10-29 — positioning, competitors, trademark (Fred 16:36–17:28)

  • Positioning: sell what happens to the group, not the technology — "the off-grid group ride app": every trail in your state, routes built for you, one ride for the group, every cart on the map with no cell signal and no monthly fee. Rider promise: "Nobody gets lost. Nobody gets left behind." Organizers / clubs / partners: built on open TAK standards (Cursor on Target), license-free mesh radios, works alongside ATAK/iTAK, no proprietary hardware or messaging (Fred 17:14: "based on the open standard TAK situational awareness protocols without use of traditional licensed infrastructure. that is our game changer"). Avoid: calling it a navigation app (onX's ground), leading with TAK/mesh to riders, naming Garmin in ads, "only" and superlatives. When the $3 subscription starts, word "no monthly fee" carefully (e.g. no fee for off-grid tracking).
  • Competitors: Meshtastic app and ATAK + Meshtastic plugin (general-purpose, no trails/routes/ride package); DirtOS — Sean Connell's app targets off-road course racing, a different universe (Fred 17:21); Garmin Tread / Rino group radio — proprietary hardware and messaging (Fred 17:24); Garmin inReach and satellite messengers — monthly subscription; onX — solo navigation, own proprietary basemap, Mapbox renderer.
  • Name idea (not decided): "GroupTrack Off Grid", logo "GroupTrack OG" (Fred 17:24). Play app-name limit 30 characters; keep "off road" in the Play title for search (e.g. "GroupTrack Off Grid Trail Maps" = 30). Trademark filing is unaffected: file GROUPTRACK.
  • Trademark: Fred 16:36 "not paying $5,000 for … trademarks on a free product". Filing yourself: USPTO Trademark Center, owner role (his USPTO.gov account shows "attorney support staff needs a sponsor" → email TEAS@uspto.gov to change the role to Trademark owner; the role is locked after identity verification). Fees (Jan 2025 schedule): $350 per class base; +$200 free-form goods text; +$100 insufficient information; +$200 per extra 1,000 characters. Worksheet (Claude Docs, "GroupTrack Trademark Application Worksheet"): standard-character GROUPTRACK, owner Fred Kix individual, Class 9 ID-Manual fill-in (planning off-road routes, offline trail maps, GPS tracks, sending group ride plans, vehicle locations in a riding group over license-free mesh radio without cellular or internet, exchanging location data with TAK Cursor-on-Target applications — ~415 characters), Section 1(a) with first-use dates to fill, specimen = Play listing screenshot with URL and access date. Search: Garmin lists no GroupTrack mark in its notices but has used GroupTrack as a one-word feature name since the Edge 820 (July 2016), now on the Tread → realistic objection point = the 30-day publication period; two 1990s filings (GROUPTRACK 75173344, GROUP-TRACK 74423372) to confirm dead; 90245541 is TRACK GROUP (Track Group Inc., class 45), unrelated. Descriptiveness answer: GROUPTRACK is one coined word used as a name (Garmin itself writes it as a capitalised feature name), fallback the Supplemental Register. Fred's prior-use evidence: Play listing, grouptrack.org, Facebook page GroupTrack.org and group. Use ™ now. Meta's ad pause for a trademark review (12:10): read the reason in Ads Manager › Account Quality; do not resubmit blindly.
  • Rejected: an "only product" claim (17:16) — false (Meshtastic app, ATAK plugin, Garmin radios share positions without fees); a TAK-heavy "defensive" Play description another AI wrote (superlatives, all caps, emojis, wrong audience, unverified features; a description does not create trademark rights).

⭐⭐ 10-28 DRIVE — RADIO LOST ON THE OUTBOUND (findings + Fred's recovery decision)

Drive 10-28 10:52–11:33, two cars (Droid 1 "fred&June" radio c46a / 601801834; Droid 2 "Billy beans" radio deda / 195288794 = !0ba3deda). Logs: ~/Downloads/gt_logs/droid1_drive_1028_1152.log (whole drive), droid2_drive_1028_1152.log (only from Droid 2's reboot ≈ 11:43 real; its clock is 2 h behind).

  • Droid 1: REC 10:52:31 (REC writes nothing to the radio). 10:52:39 the BLE link to c46a timed out (status 8) — leaving home. 8 GroupTrack reconnects (10:53–11:04) unanswered; 10:54:46 FAILCLEAR (3 failed reconnects) cleared the other carts — so Droid 2 stayed at its last heard spot (10:52:23, the start). 11:06 FORGET/re-pair: radio seen advertising but would not connect. 11:15 Fred restarted the radio at the halfway point → connected. Radio-ended drops (status 19) 11:16, 11:18; position 0,0 at 11:19 (just restarted); steady 11:19–end; heard Droid 2 moving all the way home. Bluetooth off/on on Droid 1 only at 10:40 (home).
  • Droid 2: the radio failure needed the radio restart and a tablet restart; after the restart (≈11:48) it still dropped (status 19) until FORGET + re-pair ≈11:50. Bluetooth off/on was also tried. Earlier part of its log lost in the reboot.
  • Fred's theory (12:05): caused by the radio being powered off and on without disconnecting first. Fits the log (status 8 = sudden loss, then advertising but refusing connections). In a car on USB, every engine start/stop is such a power cycle — the app has to survive it.
  • Banner bug (Main.kt ~682, LINKFIX/BANNER/POWERMSG): no way to clear it (the OK button is squeezed out of the row by the long text — most likely) and it covers the GRP Awareness button. Its 3rd-try advice (FORGET + PAIR first) did not help today.

Fred's decision (12:09): when recovery would need a tablet restart, it is unrecoverable during the ride — better to stay in Android only and keep recording the track. A tablet with no GPS chip (Droid 2) needs the restart, but that is unusual. → The in-ride recovery stops before "restart the tablet"; then Android only + REC continues.

Next (before any recovery wording): bench test, logs recording on both tablets first — A unclean power cycle while connected (wait 3 min) · B disconnect first, then power cycle · C recovery order: wait / tablet Bluetooth off-on / FORGET + re-pair / restart radio / restart tablet · D repeat with the other radio and tablet. Fred to note how each radio was powered on 10-28 and both firmware versions. The banner layout fix (OK reachable, off the GRP Awareness button) is safe now — first 2.7d patch.

An added other name obeys the same rule. Duplicates already on tablets are left alone.

D4 — IMPORT RECOVERY, designed 13:15–13:40 (Fred) — goes into 2.7e with the vector import:

  • Finding (code read 13:15): HomeStateImportController keeps a per-source record (pending / in_progress / completed / failed) and its loop skips completed sources, and findIncompleteImport() exists — but nothing calls it; every run builds a FRESH manifest (so a retry repeats everything); a killed run is stamped "killed" at start and the rider is sent back to the picker; line 622 if (allOk) "completed" else "completed" records failures (including a failed step-8 land check = public/private) as completed. Clean-up runs per state source (discardState after each Geofabrik source), not at the end of the job; the download step already reuses a verified PBF.
  • Rule (Fred): anything that did not finish picks up at the failed step; work done is kept.
  • At every start, online only (a real internet test — not "a network is connected"): a new-format record with any task not completed → message "Utah trails: 6 of 7 loaded" with three answers: Yes (complete now from the failed step, reusing downloaded/extracted files) · Next start (ask again; each skip stamped with date and time in the record) · Don't run (abandoned by rider, stamped, never offered again, its leftover files removed; trails already loaded stay). Repeats every start until done. No message without internet.
  • Unfinished = any task not completed — failed, cancelled, killed or crashed are all caught (Fred 13:37: cancelled jobs are not stamped failed — must not be missed). A cancel is also written as "cancelled" for the log.
  • record_version: 2 on every new record (+ written_by build, for logs only). Only records with record_version ≥ 2 are checked. Records saved via temp file + swap (a crash cannot leave a blank record). Line 622 fixed.
  • One-time housekeeping at first start of the new build (CODE RULE 3, no marker): old records (no record_version) — keep those whose top word is "completed" exactly as they are; remove every other old record ("in progress", "killed", unreadable/blank) with its leftover work files. Fred: "I do not want to be making sense of anything that happened prior." No reading of sources inside old records.

V3 (AWS) duplicate rules (Fred 14:05–14:13): tracks from the same ride are duplicates (by ride id); the shape test (≈95% of each line within ~15 m of the other, both directions; bbox + length prefilter; named settings) applies only across different rides. First track posted to AWS wins; later ones are rejected (no copy, no alias on AWS: "we do not want a lot of aliases on AWS"); the rider's tablet keeps its own. The ride creator's later track renames the stored track (same ride only). Routes: keep the hash on the tablet (creator-owned); on AWS one stored route, possibly with each ride creator's id as an alias.

Rejected — and why: monitoring old import records for recovery (Fred 13:25: "we are not monitoring anything prior to the pickup release"); a retry cap (message repeats until done); hash dedupe for synced tracks (3-second sampling + GPS wander → true duplicates never hash equal).

Identity. Release 2.7d is the last 2.7 release (Fred 10-27 15:21: "add to 2.7d final 2.7 version"). 2.7c goes public tonight as it is (production review submitted 10-27 afternoon); every item still open on 2.7c or earlier moves to 2.7d (Fred 15:22). After 2.7d, 2.7 is frozen and work moves to 2.8 = the native iPad app (roadmap 10-22: 2.8 ends with a deployed native iPad app; 2.9 = one-way data sync to AWS; 3.0 = Mac/PC planning station). Branch: 2.7d to be cut from the 2.7c release commit 1b63af4e0 (tag v2.7c-internal-2026-10-27-d). Emergency fixes go into 2.7d first.

Scope rule (Fred 15:24): no new scoring features; fixes, clean-up, the agreed trail and services items, the tests, and the documents.

2.7d — THE SEQUENCE (Fred 10-27 15:41: "you're in charge of the sequence … get through 2.7d as quickly as possible so we can begin 2.8")

Principle: code first, smallest first; every screen-changing item before the one capture session; documents once, at the end; tests run whenever the second rider is free. One change → build → test → commit, as always.

  1. Decide the four open questions in one sitting (10-28 morning, ~15 min). Recommendations: Feature allow-list — yes as listed (peak, spring, cliff, volcano, cave entrance score; settlements score nothing). Services layer — yes, campsites, toilets, fuel, drinking water to start; built last, so it cannot hold up the rest. Option (a) — decide only after T1; if T1 is clean, drop it. Parked radios at 20 s — I read the code and the radio config first; fix only if it is GroupTrack's setting. 19 colour boxes — I prepare the screenshots; Fred marks each in one pass.
  2. Cut branch 2.7d from 1b63af4e0.
  3. Tiny fixes, one at a time: D3 move the 7 .bak files out of assets → D4 import status line 622 → D7 Account label → D6 HELP on 6 screens → D5 Import GPX lists only .gpx / .kml.
  4. Medium fixes: D8 ride statistics saved at END → feature allow-list (if agreed) → D1 Mark unusable (trail panel + store + Create trails from tracks coverage).
  5. D2 Services layer (import the service points + map layer + FILTER line).
  6. Bench tests: T2 (Create trails from tracks again, expect 0 / 0), T3 (Droid 2 Utah panel), plus each change's own test. T1 chase test any day the second rider is free — it does not wait for the steps above.
  7. One capture session (D10) against the finished build: all 24 manual gaps (O, P, Q, email ride, CREATE RIDE, recaptures for D1/D2/D5/D6/D7/D8, Bluetooth off row) plus daily ads 2, 5, 6.
  8. Documents once: manual filled, stubs list emptied, DRAFT dropped (D9; the builder refuses a final build while stubs remain) · release notes v57 (2.7d) · Quick Start if a job changed · help links.
  9. Release: AAB → internal testing → acceptance pass → production · website docs + ads 2, 5, 6 · Play listing "What's new" · tag v2.7d. 2.7 frozen; 2.8 (native iPad app) starts.

Loose ends, done along the way: confirm the starred ad panels on the site · archive build d · post the teaser once 2.7c is live · fix the site scripts' check step (https) · the PC Drive upload problem · Droid 2's clock.

Fred 15:18: "points were supposed to be placed on trails." Code: RouteModule.onPinTap (~296) stores the tap wherever it lands; only at build time RouteExplorer (~1540) projects each pin onto the nearest trail segment and splitEdgeAt splits that trail — silently, any distance. Main suspect for both the crash (graph arrays one short after a split — e.g. two pins on one edge) and the straight line (a split edge losing its intermediate points draws a chord) — to prove 10-29. Plan: (1) snap the pin to the nearest trail AT THE TAP and show it there; (2) refuse a pin more than ~500 ft (150 m) from a trail — "No trail here — tap on a trail"; (3) warn at once if the pin's trail does not connect to the trailhead's network; (4) fix splitEdgeAt / dijkstra so a split never breaks the graph.
Fred 15:22: "I believe those checks used to exist?" — YES. The pin feasibility check (RouteExplorer.assess, rider-assisted routing stage 1, Aug) runs on every pin tap and feeds the mileage HUD's warnings ("One place is too far from any trail to reach" / "…sits on a trail that does not join the rest of the network", RouteModule ~848). The 10-28 log shows it failing on every pin drop — 20× 15:14–15:20 GuidedPin: assess failed: Index 5901 out of bounds for length 5898 — so pinFeas stays null and the warnings never show; the build then fails the same way (5949 / 5948). One indexing bug, two symptoms. Order for 10-29: (a) find in git when it broke (it worked in August) — no fix before the cause is understood; (b) fix it → the existing warnings return; (c) then decide whether snap-at-tap (steps 1–2) is still needed.

Rules and decisions 10-26:

  • The three documents support and reference each other (Fred 19:10–19:16). Quick Start = "how do I…?": common navigation and task needs, each a process combining manual steps, every step linked to its manual section. Manual = the process reference ("what does this do?"), pointing riders to the Quick Start first. Release notes = "what changed?", each entry linking to its manual section.
  • Captures: Fred captures every screen now, even ones that will change (folder marked [recapture]); Fred saves each screen with a text description straight into its folder (no automatic capture tool — retired gt_cap_v1).
  • Toggles need a folder only to show before / after; every BUTTON has its own lettered folder.
  • Trailhead rules (in the code): saving a route reuses a Trailhead within 0.1 mile of its first point, else creates one; creating a ride looks for trailheads within ½ mile; AI route design needs a trail within the trailhead's ½-mile circle.
  • The check-in choice is labelled No scheduled ride in the app; it means no survey at END, only the track name.
  • Mode switches GroupTrack between Android only and Group (not between carts) — the manual says so plainly.
  • Ride statistics email: from Rides only, rider approves by tapping Send; statistics are in memory since app start (a restart loses them; the GPX survives).
  • Config PDF: from Archived setups only; opens and emails as an attachment.

📘 2.7c manual — the process (Fred 10-24 22:04 → 10-25 00:31, written 10-25 22:40)

Aim. A 2.7c manual organised by ONE MAP's launch points. Under each launch point are the functions it launches, each a row whose screens are the steps of that function, in order, with every decision branch as its own twistie. Navigation is one row open at a time, end to end. The manual and in-app HELP are linked: each screen's HELP opens its own section (#help-<function id>). The Quick Start exercises stay the same and only their references change. The release notes list the launch points, each with a one-line service description linked to its manual section.

Fixed rules (Fred):

  • The 2.7b release manual (v65) is the source and is never changed.
  • Every 2.7b function has a 2.7c launch point: a launch point itself, or an activity under one. Only new 2.7c functions have no 2.7b text or screens.
  • Where the Planning Map and Ride Map described the same function, the MOST COMPLETE version is kept (more sub-functions, then more text, then more screens); equal versions — either.
  • Map Features is not a 2.7c function. Its panel is FILTER; its rows went to MAP KEY, FILTER, Map tasks, imports, Rides, Tap a feature and Add Route.
  • Processes such as import are unchanged: their text and screens move as they are.
  • A screen belongs to the function it shows — the function xref is the key. Match by program name, first level first: the screens invoked directly from the ONE MAP panel. Then go down the call stack, screen process by screen process.
  • MAP KEY = one display screen, and any selected row opens the same customisation screen. FILTER = one screen, plus the SELECT list.
  • GROUP and MY CART are buttons; another cart = SELECT CART, or a tap on the map. The shared frame code IS the screen. Most options are under Map, and most are one-level screens.
  • Landscape screenshots. Letters = launch points (red, 15–20); grey numbers = information points. The HUDs get separate explanations, each with its own screen.
  • Ride mode: on the standard picture, CHK IN and Add Route blink and change their titles.
  • Nothing missing or removed is mentioned.
  • "What is GroupTrack" and the explanation sections are written LAST, once the manual exists to lead into.

🛠 OPEN — an area import downloads the WHOLE state (Fred 10-24 17:06: "the killer is the 4 gig download … even for a small area")

Geofabrik ships whole states only: the area import pulls the state's PBF (read for the access tags — Fred: "we get features from the protobuf, there is a lot of data": every road, building and node of the state) and its GeoPackage, parses all of it, keeps the box. AREASKIP / AREAPICK stop it for states already imported whole, not for a new one. Options (Claude 17:06): (1) for an AREA import fetch only the box from OpenStreetMap's query service (Overpass) — the box's trails WITH the same access tags the PBF supplies, MB not GB; whole-state imports stay on Geofabrik; public Overpass has fair-use limits and refuses very large boxes → fall back to (2); (2) keep the state's reduced extract after a first download so later area imports in that state read it locally; (3) warn with the size before downloading. Recommended 1 + 2. To decide: an open 2.7c item, or after the tester version.

Fred: re-mark instead of delete, so they are not shown or used and not recreated — category "unofficial/uncertain". Design (Claude 17:01): a deleted trail from tracks is re-marked unofficial/uncertain, keeps its exact shape; the rebuild's shape match then finds it and does not lay it again; the rebuild's stale clean-up removes only rows still marked rider, so it stays; unofficial/uncertain is never drawn, searched, routed or counted. Limit: new trail data near it can change the piece's shape — then it reappears once. Also offered: a TRACK-level mark ("don't make trails from this track") for test recordings. 🧭 Route+ on ONE MAP — the module and its rules (built 10-23)

The design (Fred 13:09–13:39). Route+ is lifted out of the planner into ONE module both maps could call (grouptrack/RouteModule.kt: GtRoute = all state in one place; GtRouteHost = everything Route+ shows). The planner's code was copied, not retrofitted (09:06 method) — the planner keeps its own until it is removed. Route drafts stay SHARED (RouteDraftStore, no map name); RouteState is per map (ONE MAP writes it under "grouptrack", and only for a hand-built route). Manual and AI have their OWN existence checks and recoveries — mutually exclusive (Fred 13:39). One mode is launched, locked; the other is not shown (13:35).

Entry order (Fred 13:18, 13:35, 14:29–14:34, 14:43): open AI compare set → the COMPARE TABLE · an UNNAMED route (a crash) → loaded, building carries on, no prompt · NAMED routes in progress (even one) → the selector: open one, + Build it myself, + AI route design · nothing → the Route panel. Leftover AI routes are never deleted at launch — only from the compare table. AI either opens the compare table or starts a new build.

Build it myself: trails-only snap 15–150 m by zoom, per-point auto-save, the planner's toolbar locked to one mode, opened top centre below RECORD above the launch buttons (drag by its grip); Save (name, trailhead check), Discard, ✕ always leaves, Back held. Crash pick-up and the selector redraw the line at once (GTROUTE1G).

AI route design: overview (1/6, opt-out box) and distance and speed (2/6) as a centred half-height panel, fonts ≥ 14 · trailhead step with the aim ring and ONE MAP's SEARCH inside the panel behind a twistie (no keyboard until the box is tapped; a picked result folds it) · loop or finish pin · own places (max 5, mileage HUD, reachability) · summary · FIND MY RIDES → the compare table, fitted to the new routes. Step panels in ONE MAP's colour scheme with HELP ✕. Filter rules (Fred 15:39–15:44): AI turns Trails ALL and Waypoints ALL on and keeps them on (again at every trailhead step); the compare table uses the filter's own HIDE EVERYTHING plus off-grid areas off, and restores exactly on Exit / Save. Said on the overview.

Compare table: show / hide each route, tick keepers, SAVE (area name → "<area> 1", "<area> 2"; the rest deleted), none ticked → "Delete all routes?"; EXIT (✕) leaves the set open; the map returns to the previous view.

Not copied from the planner, on purpose: ROUTEPLUSCLEAN / deleteOrphanAiDrafts at Route+ and at AI start, the unnamed-draft delete at AI start and after a compare save (on ONE MAP the unnamed draft is a crash pick-up), the planner's AI results panel between the search and the compare table.

  • Restore maps as of a date (the replay must be AREA jobs at REFRESH priority) — "Archived maps" is its date picker.
  • Imported trails / features by state — a list: state, date, trails, sources, private count. Fred 10-24 12:39: the row reads "Display trail, waypoint counts by state". DONE — f2397971b (AREASKIP-2026-10-24): counts come from the import JSONs (manifests in imports/ and imports/history/) — trails and points are not stamped with a state on the tablet.
  • DONE — f2397971b — AREASKIP-2026-10-24 (Fred 10-24 12:40-12:45): the AREA import that crosses state lines skips downloading and reprocessing a state that has already been imported WHOLE. The area import picks its states by lat / long (GeofabrikCatalog.findByBbox); a state with a completed whole-state import JSON (area.type "state", its Open Source Maps source completed) is bypassed, and so is a catalog source that state import already loaded whole. Fred 12:45: "if OSM includes the state we omit it from OSM processing" — the area OSM step downloads the WHOLE state's PBF (~167 MB) and GeoPackage (~332 MB), parses it, then keeps only the box's trails; a skipped state gets none of that. The run shows "Already imported whole — skipped: …"; the manifest records "skipped". AREAPICK-2026-10-24 (Fred 12:53): picking states by rectangle pulls states with no ground in the box (California North on the Nevada line, measured on a S-Utah / NV / AZ box) — so the area import now shows the STATE LIST first: imported-whole states shown and skipped, every other state ticked, untick to leave it out, then START (run as THE import). Fred 12:54: Utah Trails and Pathways is small and is simply processed again. OPEN: whole-state sources in an area run — another issue (Fred 12:54).
  • Draw bar with sources and Replace, saved defaults — also covers Download Y/N per source (map_slots.json; default tick in the confirm builders).
  • CLOSED 10-24 13:16 — DECIDED, NOT BUILT: GroupTrack's own Downloads picker. Fred: "too complicated, leave the whole thing as is." Import a ride and Import GPX keep the Android file picker. Why (so it is not rediscovered): Android 11+ has NO one-time grant to Download like GPS / radio — All Files Access is the only one (refused by Google, appeal rejected 09-11); READ storage no longer reaches other apps' files; a folder grant cannot be the Download folder itself, only a folder inside it (Download/GroupTrack) — and Gmail saves attachments to Download itself, so riders would have to move files. Filtering the Android picker narrows by MIME type only, not extension (email apps label .gpx / .convoy as octet-stream). onX does the same: My Content › Import › choose your file (Android file chooser); KML on the web map. "Open with GroupTrack" from the email already works (release g, .convoy filter). Fred 13:11: no share function beyond Downloads (no Drive, no mapped drives). The tested patch is ~/Downloads/patch_gt_file_picker_2026-10-24_DECIDED_NOT_TO_BUILD_DO_NOT_RUN.py.
  • CLOSED 10-24 13:06: whole-state sources being rerun in area functions — AREASKIP + AREAPICK v2 (the list of everything the area run processes, whole-imported states unticked).

📻 BTECH Relay — a transport candidate for 2.8+ (Nathan via Fred, 10-23 16:04)

github.com/darksteal/BTECH-Relay — an ATAK plugin that links BTECH UV-PRO / GMRS-PRO / UV-50X radios to ATAK over Bluetooth SPP + the radio's KISS TNC; AX.25 frames, a compact binary format with fragmentation, optional AES-256; position, chat, CoT markers / routes, contact age-out and Bluetooth auto-reconnect working, voice PTT a scaffold; 2 commits. Nathan: he has run a different networking stack over the UV-Pro, "the solution is pretty sound".

Fit: UTV riders already carry GMRS radios. 47 CFR 95.1787 lets a GMRS handheld send location (or a request for it) and brief text — each burst ≤ 1 s, no more than once per 30 s, integral antenna, not on the 467 MHz main channels — a GroupTrack position beacon fits; tracks / routes / markers do not. Packet modems typically 1200 baud. A transport behind the 2.8 separation beside Meshtastic and the Nucleus IP mesh.

Cautions: licence MIT + Commons Clause (commercial sale reserved) — do not copy their code into the paid app without the author's permission; KISS / AX.25 / SPP are open standards, write our own. Encryption is not addressed in 95.1787; amateur rules prohibit messages encoded to obscure meaning — check before any encrypted traffic on these bands. Needs real radios and distance (January).

Next: ask Nathan which stack he used over the UV-Pro and the throughput he saw.

📐 Rules set on 10-22 (Fred) — they govern every patch from here

  1. One standard for leaving: HELP ✕ top right on every screen, panel and dialog. ✕ = leave (close / cancel; nothing else happens). The COMPLETE is the screen's own action button (CHECK IN, SAVE, APPLY, SEND). Panels that stay on screen add ☰ MOVE and HIDE / SHOW. One help viewer (GtHelpView) opens on top of every screen. (Fred 12:18–12:21: "no preference as long as it is consistent.")
  2. The touch rule (12:06, 12:50): every screen we have touched is brought to the new standard — scroll, HELP, ✕ — even if the feature is a placeholder; when a function is touched, every screen it opens is brought to the standard in the same patch.
  3. Nothing is removed without a home (11:47): a control is never hidden or replaced unless the same patch puts it somewhere. The hidden-controls ledger below records every one.
  4. The one-option rule (11:09): a button whose module has one attached process runs it directly — no in-between list. Key functions are never hidden between navigation layers (11:07).
  5. Bring over exactly as it exists, then restructure (14:35): moving a function never redesigns it; realignment comes after everything is moved.
  6. Automated is not a menu item (15:10): a function that only runs inside another process (corridor downloads after an import, a source refresh) moves with its process and never gets a button.
  7. Trace before attaching (Claude, after the Add Route detour 14:33): a function that crosses into another screen is traced start → work → complete → cancel → back before it is handed over, and the dead ends are reported first.
  8. Route+ is not taken apart (13:48–13:58): it is moved whole; its start-up checks (in-progress routes, compare sets, leftover AI routes, the unnamed draft) always run.
  9. Search filters after, not before (13:04): search everything; the result types are the filter.
  10. Map selection and map sources belong together (15:10).
  11. Persistence purpose (13:30–13:33): the state file is for returning BETWEEN SESSIONS to where you left off. Start-up ALWAYS restores the saved view — no exceptions; Centre on Me is one tap to where you are. ("If startup centres me where I am it is a lot of work to return to where I was. If I return to where I was it is one button to bring me to where I am.")
  12. Attaching order (11:01): top row, down the right column, then the bottom bar left to right.

🗺 Roadmap (decided 10-22 in the open-items review)

ReleaseScope
2.7cONE MAP: navigation, simplification, the presentation layer including 3D / terrain and rotation, the manual rebuilt by a repeatable process. Portrait fully workable; landscape is riding mode.
2.8Ends with a deployed native iPad app; the Apple work folded together.
2.9Data-model sync and upload from Android to AWS — one direction only, services layer only, no paygate.
3.0AWS services: the paygate (Play promo codes — e.g. the first 200 users free for a year), Mac / PC planning against the AWS model, the map-licensing review (stay within Esri / Google / OpenTopoMap terms; corridors over HTTP).
4.0Tile brokering / staging DROPPED — "no benefit to the server deciding, as the app does this task already" on download and import.

2.7c method (Fred 10-22): a blank launch panel with every final launch point as a placeholder, then functions attached to the scaffolding; one module per launch point; the last step retires ConvoyScreen.kt and GroupTrack.kt becomes the start screen. 2.7c standards: every panel scrolls · one colour scheme (buttons and panels follow the map type: SAT / HYB white with blue letters, otherwise blue with white) · detail panels separate facts from actions and hide technical data (done one panel at a time in the reviews) · stay-panels have drag, collapse, complete, help · every launch point and function links to its manual pages through a data table the documentation process regenerates (grouptrack_help_links.json).

⭐⭐⭐ GroupTrack 2.7c — ONE MAP — THE TARGET (locked 2026-10-21 13:24, Fred)

v1 · 2026-10-21 · Built from the design canvas "GroupTrack One Map — launch layer prototype" (claude.ai artifact 8SH4mp7qiLLuUuFFHQDutx, worked through with Fred 12:15–13:19) and checked against docs/navigation_xref.txt (generated 2026-10-20 17:14, branch 2.7b @ c176d4026: 143 panels, 510 clickable functions). Every one of the 510 functions has a home in this target (section 6).

1. The decision: freeze 2.7b, and One Map becomes 2.7c

2.7b is frozen in scope (Fred 13:23). It ships once three things are done:

  1. the estimate / gap-fill rule (desk test, then patch);
  2. the cleanup patch: remove the POLYTEST test button, fix the duplicate VM listener, fix the COMMHEALTH foreground-service start while the app is in the background;
  3. 2.7b release notes, manual and Quick Start.

Moved to 2.7c: polyline statistics, reconnect panel v4, and the polyline-dots question. The two-person field test waits for Utah in January (Fred decides whether 2.7b goes to internal testers before it).

Why separate releases:

  • 2.7b changed how positions flow (the tick removed, lead track, gap polyline, trim, estimate); 2.7c changes how riders move around the app. Each needs its own testing and release notes, or a trail problem cannot be traced to one change.
  • 2.7c rewrites every lettered control in manual sections 4 and 5.
2.7c phaseWhatEstimate (not a commitment)
S1Inventory: confirm each launch point and the chain it opens today (this document is the starting list)1 day
1 · ShellThe new layout on both maps (control placement, two layouts, panels, KEY, FILTER, INTERNET, readout, distance, queue badge, landscape). Panel items first open TODAY'S screens. Test: every row of section 6 is reachable.1–2 weeks
2 · Quick winsMap type as one choice; INTERNET switch; GPS rate inline; roles in the list; CHECK IN + REC as one step; END RIDE screen; Search opens directly; flyover in Map≈ 1 week
3 · Big simplificationsKEY styles-only and FILTER OFF/ALL/SELECT (map drawing code); Create a ride; Download for offline; Import trail data; Create a route (planner questions); My rides; Import a ride; Import GPX2–3 weeks
4 · One mapFold the planning map in as panels, then retire the planning page (route state, draft resolver, frame restore — the riskiest step, last)1–2 weeks
DocsManual sections 4 and 5 rewritten to this layout2–3 days

Total ≈ 6–9 weeks at the current pace; phase 4 is the least certain.

2. Principles (Fred, 10-18 to 10-21)

  • One map. The planning map is folded in; the Ride Planning / Back to Ride switches are retired.
  • A launch layer over the existing features. Each process gets ONE first screen that takes every value it needs (defaults from the ride and settings), then runs to the end with no further screen prompts. The only stop kept is the confirm before something that cannot be undone.
  • Two layouts. Group adds a lot of complexity and is used by some. Android only is the default; Group adds the Group button, CHECK IN, the GROUP readout, other carts and the lead track. One switch decides which.
  • Riders ride in landscape and pan in portrait. In landscape, panels open as a side panel so the map stays in view.
  • Every control shows an icon and its text on an opaque background, readable on any map. No hover, no long-press meanings.
  • North up only. Leaflet cannot rotate, and Hybrid labels are baked into the imagery (heading-up needs a rotating map engine: not 2.7c).
  • Show/hide is FILTER, how lines look is KEY: as onX and Gaia do, the filter is on every view.

3. The screen: where every control sits

PlaceControl (top to bottom / left to right)What it does
Top centreGPS light · zoom levelInformation only. GPS: green "GPS LOCK n" = a fix from n satellites; red = still looking; the no-chip wording is open (section 8).
Top centre, under itRECORD buttonCHECK IN (Group: ride, role, radio on one screen, then records) · REC (Android only: records at once) · END RIDE while recording.
Left columnMENUSettings (incl. Group riding, Storage & data, Diagnostics) · Help (the three documents) · Account.
Left columnZoom + / −
Right column 1ANDROID ONLY / GROUPFlips the whole layout. The same value as MENU › Settings › Group riding. Blocked while recording ("End the ride first").
Right column 2INTERNET ON / OFFShows the state; tap to go LOCAL (only what is on the tablet).
Right column 3KEYThe legend, styles only.
Right column 4FILTERWhat shows: OFF / ALL / SELECT per feature type, and Hide.
Right column 5CENTRE ON MECentres on your cart, north up.

Bottom rightDISTANCE TRAVELEDThis ride while recording, else the last ride.

On the mapTap a feature · long press · tap a cartFeature details · New waypoint · (Group) that cart's readout.

The same placement holds in portrait and landscape (tablet 1280 × 800). In landscape the panels and first screens open from the right edge.

5. Found by the cross-reference: functions the prototype did not show, and their homes

  • MENU › Settings › Storage & data: Remove all trail data · Resync tracks · Add trails from tracks (from Settings, the track import screen and the RiderTrails dialog).
  • MENU › Settings › Diagnostics: Check a trail · Ride statistics · Conversion record (from Settings and the old ride-config menu).
  • Map › Map sources: change a source (clear or keep tiles: the migrate gate) and rebuild a source (MAP RECOVERY / recreate slot).
  • Map › Download for offline: show what you hold / what is coming, and remove tiles from an area.
  • Rides › Create a route: drafts in progress (resume, rename, roll back, delete, the unnamed-draft resolver), ROUTE DETAILS notes, CREATE TRAILHEAD at save, the planner's Review / Compare and saving the compare set.
  • Tap a feature: rename, change type, delete, fit, aliases, Create a ride; tracks add SHARE (the track export sheet).
  • Record button: the automatic-pause banner (CONTINUE), the ROLE CONFLICT banner, the lead question when no Leader is known (decide in phase 2).
  • Unchanged in 2.7c: Android permission prompts, the startup online/offline chooser, sign-in, terms and privacy gates, the V3 account and organizer screens, the developer menu.

6. Every function in today's panels → its new home

Coverage check: 143 panels and 510 clickable functions in navigation_xref.txt (2026-10-20, c176d4026). Functions without a home: 0. Status counts: MERGED 155, KEPT 154, MOVED 107, UNCHANGED 79, RETIRED 13, SPLIT 2.

KEPT = same function, reached from its new place · MOVED = same function, new place · MERGED = folded into a first screen · RETIRED = goes away · UNCHANGED = not part of 2.7c · SPLIT = its functions go to different homes (see 6.2). Labels are as the cross-reference records them; a few sit one line off their button.
